# build-progress — BabyBloom London (code repo ledger)

> **The repo's own build ledger** (`CLAUDE.md` §9; `08-launch-and-cutover.md` §2.1 step 0 (v); SEQUENCE `14.33`). Must always reflect the true current state so any fresh context resumes perfectly. Updated whenever code state changes; bumped before every compaction (`CLAUDE.md` §8). Task-level tracking lives in `../OPERATIONS/ACTIVE/L-NNN-…/PROGRESS.md`; merged units are one line each in `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Known bugs / gaps (recorded, not hidden)

- **AUTH-2 (L-007, 2026-09-17) closed `1c`'s pins (3) (4) (6), wired `positions` at boot, and confirmed ADR-135.** **ADR-135 needed no code:** S5b had already built `/api/health`'s 503 directly in the route with the body unchanged, flipped E1's smoke assertion to 503 + `db: "failed"` and kept the 200 + `ok` unit test — measured green on this branch, nothing to change. **`positions` is wired:** `src/boot/wire-positions.ts` installs `configurePositions(createPositions({ store }))` **and** `registerPositionsSlice(createPositionsSlice({ store, isInServiceArea }))` over **one** `memoryPositionStore`, outside production only — the store is per instance and a cold start would drop a parent's open position silently, so production stays on `positions-not-configured` with its reason on the report; the db store over `nanny_positions` (`0006`) is still owed. **07 §8 row 3 is paid on S-X-09:** the reset request is limited per **hashed** address before any send, over the limit answers `ok` and sends nothing (a throttle that showed would be the oracle again), and a limiter outage **refuses rather than sends** — deliberately the opposite of the public _read_ surfaces, which is the rule `consume-public-read-limit.ts` itself states.
- **S5b (L-007, 2026-09-17) closed ADR-130 and ADR-131, and paid 07 §8 row 1.** **ADR-131 (1):** `TableQuery` / `ViewQuery` carry `eq(column, value)` → a read-only `KeyedRead` (`select()` · `single()`); a keyed write is a compile error whatever name the handle was reached through, so ADR-129's "views stay read-only" needs no runtime check on that path. The three corners P1-WIRE had to leave dark are live: `email_logs` + `inbox_messages` (`dbCommsStore`, wired), the `cookie_consent_records` half of consent (read **and** write), and `events` reads on the two indexed access paths — an **unkeyed** `queryEvents` is still refused (`event-log-read-requires-key`): a whole-table scan under the service role is not a read model, and `countByName` stays closed for the same reason (an aggregate has no key; it belongs over 02 §7's views). **ADR-131 (2) / (3) + `1c`'s owed definer:** migration `0017`. **ADR-130:** `/api/health` answers **503** when the probe fails, body unchanged — a 200 with a dead dependency is a monitor that never fires. **07 §8 row 1** now applies to `/api/areas` and the quick-match API over the shared store, keyed on the **hashed** caller address (never the consent-gated `visitor_id` — §2.9 / §8 row 4).
- **★ S5b: `record_cookie_consent` had two bugs, and only one was found by reading.** The `database-reviewer` (0 CRITICAL · 1 HIGH · 3 MEDIUM · 3 LOW, approve-with-warnings) caught the race: `FOR UPDATE` does not serialise a supersede under READ COMMITTED, so a double-click on the cookie banner made the second caller supersede nothing and collide on `cookie_consent_records_current_idx` — now an advisory lock per visitor. Writing the functional suite its LOW-3 asked for then found the other one on the **first run**: the function inserted the new row while the old one was still current, so the unique index refused it even single-threaded. The happy path was broken and 151 passing metadata tests had no way to see it. Stamping first is the only safe order and needs the FK deferred, which `0017` now does. Recorded here because it is the unit's clearest evidence for "a claim the merge rests on ships as an executable test" — this claim had shipped as prose and been reviewed by a specialist before it was run.
- **★ S5b: a limiter outage lets a public read through, loudly — owed to BAI for ratification.** `consumePublicReadLimit` logs `ALERT_PROVIDER_DOWN` and proceeds when `rate_limit_buckets` cannot answer, because 07 §8 is two layers and the edge rule in front of these routes does not depend on our database: refusing here would turn one database blip into an outage of the public front door. The choice lives in **one file** and is scoped to unauthenticated **reads** — a write, a booking or an auth surface must not adopt it by copying that file. 07 §8 does not say which way this should go; this is S5b's reading, not the document's.
- **S5b: 02 §3's enum register owes a `consent_purpose` row.** The code register carries it (80 enums, ordinals frozen, snapshot updated) and `enum-ordinals.test.ts` judges it against the applied schema, but S5b's brief scoped its `SPECS/` edits to the changed table rows and the `0017` row in §6, so §3's table is unchanged. **Owner: the 02 owner**, one row.
- **S5b: `book_slot()` was already built.** The brief asked S5b to create it in `0017`; `0009_scheduling.sql` created it in S5 (02 §6 names it there). Nothing was duplicated. What `scheduling` still lacks is an inside — see the `1d` entry below; the keyed read removes one of the two reasons it could not be built, but the one-uow-one-RPC rule still refuses the table writes around `book_slot`, so replacing 1d's stub `book` path is **not** a one-line swap and was not attempted.

- **★ 1d: `platform`'s `piiSafeString` refuses ~15 % of random uuids** — measured 2 944 / 20 000 (`crypto.randomUUID()` → `scrubFreeText` redacts a 9+ digit run that spans the hyphens as a phone number). 03 §9.3 says `P.id` is "a uuid or an opaque id". Under a unit of work an event whose `bookingId` / `positionId` is such a uuid fails the whole transition — `chooseSlot` would fail one time in seven in production. **Owned by `platform`** (shared module; not edited by 1d). Pinned as `it.fails` in `call-layer.inside.test.ts`; the 1d suites pin the uuid mint to stay deterministic. Fix candidate: accept a canonical uuid before the scrub check.
- **1d: `scheduling`'s inside is still F-b's stub.** 03 §1.4's `Query` has no keyed read or filter (`select` reads a whole table), so `getBooking` / `listForSubject` / holds cannot be built over the data port; `book_slot()` exists but the one-uow-one-RPC rule (P1-WIRE) refuses the table writes around it. The displacement path (03 §3.3 I-2 / I-3 / I-13; §3.5 seq 3) is **not built** — pinned `it.fails`, not invented; a parent who taps a `displaceable` slot sees "That time has just gone" (the stub answers `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, mapped to the taken message).
- **1d: `call-layer.findOpenCall(parentId)` is a connector extension** (03 §2.7 has no "which call is mine" read; the call page cannot be reached without one). Amend-first: raised for ratification in the L-007 `1d` entry. The mirror store port (`CallMirrorStore`) is the inside's; the implementation over `nanny_positions` (0006's four call columns) waits on the RPC opener + `positions`' inside (1e).
- **1d: `getJourneySteps` is keyed by `ParentId` while a session carries a `UserId`** (02 §4 gives `parents` its own id). `loadParentJourney` passes the user id through in one place; 1e settles the key. The nanny's `call-confirmation` + `admin-commission-booking` at `openNannyCall` are not sent (no road to a nanny's email; events are emitted).
- **P1-FIX (L-007, 2026-09-17) closed ADR-119 / ADR-121 / ADR-128:** `TransitionHandler` · `SliceRegistration` · `RegisterSlice` now live in `shared-types/stage-model.ts` (the `connections` / `placements` local copies deleted; `positions` keeps the one `registerSlice` implementation); `/api/health` answers `{ data: { sha, env, db }, requestId }` + `x-request-id` with `db` a real fail-closed read through the data port (E1's item 3 below and its `test.fail()` are closed — the smoke asserts `db: "failed"` because it boots with Supabase unreachable by design; `route.test.ts` pins `db: "ok"` against `stub-auth`); the three monitoring names are `○` everywhere and `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` joined the registry (51 names). - **★ 1c: `user_profiles` has no definer, so a signup cannot complete on the real schema.** 02 §4.1 puts the row in the signup action ("exactly one `user_roles` + one `user_profiles` row per user"); ADR-127 makes any application write a SECURITY DEFINER function; `0000`–`0016` carry none for it (S4's gap, restated with its owner). `onboarding-parent` holds the write behind a fail-closed `ParentProfileStore` registry (`configureParentProfileStore`) — **migration owed:** `create_parent_profile(first_name, last_name, mobile)` writing the caller's own row (`user_id = auth.uid()`, C-7 CHECK, `email` mirrored from `auth.users`) — then one `src/boot/wire-parent-profile-store.ts` line over `auth.data.run` → `rpc(...)`. Pinned RED: `onboarding-parent.signup.test.ts` "PINNED (02 §4.1; migration owed)".
- **~~★ 1c: three things the signup surfaces need that `auth`'s connector does not have~~ — (1), (2) and (4) CLOSED by AUTH-2 (2026-09-17).** **(1) the anonymous passwordless catch** and **(2) `requestPasswordReset(email)`** are one method, because ADR-132 rules that from outside they must be indistinguishable: `auth.requestPasswordReset` answers the **same `Result` for every address** and the only difference is what the account receives. `1c`'s pin (3) is closed **but not as written** — it asked the login form to route a known passwordless email to `/set-password`, which is itself the enumeration oracle ADR-132 forbids; recorded in the test file rather than quietly rewritten. **(4) the recovery session** now reaches `/reset-password`: `gateDecision` allows one path for one session class, `GateSession.isRecovery` coming from the provider's own `amr`, never a query string. **Still open: (3) `signUp` cannot tell a duplicate email from an outage** — ADR-132 rules it no longer needs to at the form, but `onboarding-parent.signup.test.ts`'s pin still spells the old behaviour and wants its owner's ruling to flip or delete.

- **1a: `VisitorTracker` + `/api/analytics/visit` are gone** (they wrote `page_visits`, never created on the fresh DB — P-6). The London beacon is `platform`'s client `track` → `POST /api/events`, not built (01 §7).
- **★ E1: five things the assembled app measurably does that the foundations do not say — the document wins in each, none bent to match the code.**
  1. **`check:prod-guard` was green for the wrong reason, and E1 fixed it.** `prod-guard-boot.sh` started `next start` with `NODE_ENV=production` and an inherited environment in which `VERCEL_ENV` is unset. `resolveEnvironment` then reads **production**, whose column requires four names CI never sets (`VERCEL_ENV` · `NEXT_PUBLIC_META_PIXEL_ID` · `META_CAPI_ACCESS_TOKEN` · `META_DATASET_ID`), and `parseEnv` throws on those **before** `refineEnv` — where the `stub-stripe` rule actually lives — is reached. The boot exited non-zero, the required `build` check reported OK, and **07 §5.5 item 2 was never exercised**. Measured by running `parseEnv` under each shape: `VERCEL_ENV` unset → `[NEXT_PUBLIC_META_PIXEL_ID, VERCEL_ENV, META_CAPI_ACCESS_TOKEN, META_DATASET_ID]`; `VERCEL_ENV=production` + `stub-stripe` → `[PURCHASE_PROVIDER, STUB_EVENT_SECRET]`, which is the guard. The script now takes its environment from `scripts/ci/lib/smoke-env.sh` in production mode, so the only fault left is the provider; **mutation-checked** — removing the stub provider and its secret makes the guard report FAIL (the server boots and serves).
  2. **An off-Vercel production runtime cannot boot at all, and two documents disagree about whether it should.** 07 §5.5 item 2 and ADR-108 name "off-Vercel `NODE_ENV = production` at runtime" as a production resolution; 06 §2.5 marks `VERCEL_ENV` **required** in the prod column. Together they make that runtime unbootable — `resolveEnvironment` only returns production off-Vercel when `VERCEL_ENV` is absent, and the prod column then rejects its absence. Pinned as measured behaviour in `boot-guard.sh` case 6 so the shape change is visible the day someone resolves it. **Decision owed:** either mark `VERCEL_ENV` optional in the prod column, or amend 07 §5.5 to say production is Vercel-only.
  3. **`/api/health` does not answer what the foundations say it answers.** 06 §7.3: "`/api/health` is a route handler (01 §4e) returning `{ data: { sha, env, db: "ok" } }` after one trivial Supabase read; no auth; rate-limited"; 06 §4.5's promote step verifies "200 **with the new sha**"; HANDOFF §11 G2 repeats it; 01 §4c puts `requestId` on every envelope. The shipped route returns a bare `{ status: "ok" }` — no envelope, no `sha`, no `env`, no db read, no `x-request-id`, no rate limit — a deliberate F-c choice ("the least informative route in the app") that **no ADR records**. E1 wrote the assertion to the document and marked it `test.fail()` rather than rewrite it to match the code (`tests/e2e/shell-smoke/boot-and-health.spec.ts`). **Decision owed:** fix the route, or write the ADR that exempts it — and if the ADR, 06 §7.3, 06 §4.5 and HANDOFF §11 G2 are the sections to amend first. Until then the promote runbook's verify step cannot be performed as written.
  4. ~~**`ALERT_ENV_INVALID` logs `envNames: "[redacted]"`.**~~ **Closed by P1-WIRE** — the key is `invalidEnvVars`, pinned in `src/boot/__tests__/boot.test.ts`. (F-c's security review added the failing env **names** to the boot alert; `platform/log`'s scrubber redacted them by key, `env_names` matching `name(e?s)?`. Observed in a real failed boot.)
  5. **The legacy `/signup` page's top heading is an `h2`** — there is no `h1` on the page. Found because the smoke asserts the `(auth)` group renders. A11y, 05 §8.2 / AC-Y, owned by F-d / Phase 1, not fixable from a smoke suite.
- **E1, recorded not fixed, and not a foundations disagreement:** a **forged** Stripe signature answers 500 `INTERNAL`, not 400/422 `E_EVENT_UNVERIFIED`, because `payments` has no inside and its registry fails closed before signature verification is reached. Fail-closed is correct today (nothing forged is ever answered 2xx, which is what the smoke asserts), but a provider retries a 500 forever — whoever writes the payments inside should re-assert this case as `E_EVENT_UNVERIFIED` per 03 §5.4.
- **★ S5's foundations defects and open questions, all measured, none guessed.** (1) ~~**`availability_rules.weekday`**~~ **— ruled 2026-09-16: 02 §4.4 is authoritative, `Mon = 0`**, which is what `0009` wrote. `config/scheduling.ts` (`weekdays: [1,2,3,4,5] // (0 = Sunday)`) is the side to be corrected in a later unit; S5 did not touch it. The measurement is worth keeping: seeding from the config array under 02's convention would have produced **Tue–Sat**, silently. (2) **02 §4's table count is one out** — its own per-cluster list adds to 57 (56 in `public` + `auth.users`), the stated total says 56; the per-cluster numbers are right and `0016` asserts those. (3) **`rate_limit_buckets` has no spec** — 07 §8 names the table, 02 §4/§6 never create it, so S5 did not invent its columns; auth-surface rate limiting stays dark until it is specified. (4) **`file_retention_log`** — same shape (07 §6.2 row 16 names it as "new table — §11 item 8"), not created. (5) **`admin_notifications.kind` is missing `abuse_flagged`** (07 §8) and `booking_flagged` (07 §5.4 row 6) — 02 §3's enum has 12 values and `shared-types` matches; the enum is add-only so this is one migration whenever 02 §3 gains them. (6) **07 §6.2's `set_config('app.retention_job', …)`** is not the control and must not be written as though it were — the exemption is ownership by `bbldn_retention` (see the append-only note above), and a function-level `SET app.job = …` is refused on this platform anyway. (7) **The `SYSTEM_PARENT_ID` system account** (06 §2.5, HANDOFF §6.3) is **not created**: a migration cannot read an env var, and 06 calls the uuid "regenerated" per environment, so fixing one in DDL would contradict it. Nothing in Phase 0b reads it. (8) **Three of D-6's four UK-vocabulary CHECKs are deferred** — only `MATCHING.qualificationLadder` exists in config; the other three lists are Phase 2 verification research, and inventing their values in DDL would put a product fact in a migration (L4). The columns exist, so that migration is ALTER-only. (9) **The ONS attribution the site must carry** (OGL v3, three lines — `_work/areas/README.md` §7) is a Phase 3 legal-page item; the data is in the schema, the credit is not yet on a page. (10) **The nine boundary-sliver districts are seeded `borough = null`** — the dataset marks them single-source, and ADR-101 makes `borough` display-only, so the conservative reading is not to display what only one source supports; the `database-reviewer` additionally disputes BR8 / CR3 / CR6 / WD3, which the dataset marks verified and this unit therefore left alone. (11) **`hold` can never displace** — a hold _is_ a `bookings` row (02 §5 row 9), so a parent cannot hold a nanny-occupied slot, and 03 §3.5 seq 1's hold→confirm→book therefore has no displacement path; either `hold` displaces or the module skips `hold` for a displaceable slot. Contract question for 03 §3.
- **`message_status` gained `deduped` mid-unit** (coordinator ruling, 2026-09-16; 02 §3 amended to match): seven values, `dry_run` staying snake_case. `0000` and `shared-types/enums/comms.ts` moved together and the ordinal snapshot took a one-line deliberate update. `0000` was **amended rather than superseded by a `0017`** — the rule against editing an applied migration protects history that exists, and this set has never merged and has run only on a disposable preview and a local stack, both re-applied from empty afterwards to prove it.
- **`connection_stage` has no canonical live / terminal split.** 02 §4.2 row 7 requires "≤ 1 **live** per (position, nanny)" but neither 02 §3, 03 §2 nor `shared-types/stage-model.ts` says which values are live. `0007` takes "live = not terminal" with nine terminal branches named in the file, and treats `NANNY_APPLIED`, `AWAITING_RESPONSE` and `INTRO_INCOMPLETE` as live. If the stage-model owner rules otherwise the fix is one migration replacing two partial indexes.
- **`book_slot()` does not generate slots.** 03 §3.6 makes `generate-slots.ts` / `next-free-slot.ts` pure TypeScript shared by the real inside and `scheduling.stub.ts`; re-implementing the window, DST and grid rules in PL/pgSQL would be a second source of truth for I-6 and I-7 that could drift silently. The function does the part only the database can do atomically — lock the calendar, honour the hold, displace under I-2 / I-3 / I-13, insert — and the caller emits the events (03 §3.6: "scheduling writes the row, the caller emits").

- **★ CLOSED by P1-WIRE (2026-09-17) — `src/instrumentation.ts` wires every port that has a real inside, over the ADR-127 opener.** F-c had created the file for the env guard alone (kept: an invalid env still **exits**, `check:prod-guard` + `check:boot-guard` green); S4 / S5 / F-c all deferred the ports on one KEY decision, which ADR-127 made — _the transaction opener is the database function boundary; one RPC is one transaction._ The boot file now calls `src/boot/wire-ports.ts` and logs one `boot: port wired` line per port (`binding` + `reason`). What each port got, and the three corners deliberately left closed — each with an executable test (ADR-120 rule 1: `src/boot/__tests__/boot.test.ts` runs `register()` against a valid preview env and proves every binding on the module-level connector; `ports.fail-closed.test.ts` proves the defaults with the wiring absent):
  - **unit of work → `rpcTransactionOpener`** (`platform/unit-of-work/`, the six uow files moved out of `platform/lib/`): a ledger, never a connection — `begin` mints a row, `claim` books the one RPC, `commit` / `rollback` close it and touch no database (the function committed at the RPC boundary). **`auth.data.run(op, { uow })` now honours a token** through the binding's `join` (`AuthDeps.unitOfWork`, defaulting to `platform.unitOfWorkJoin`): judged before the driver is touched (`unit-of-work-unknown`), one `rpc()` (a second → `second-rpc-in-unit-of-work`), reads pass, a table `insert` / `update` refused (`write-outside-rpc` — under PostgREST it could never be atomic with the RPC), a failing RPC back as `INTERNAL`, never a throw. S4's `unit-of-work-not-supported` refusal is superseded and its test re-pinned to the document.
  - **auth → `supabaseAuthDriver` + the boot join.** No env name selects `stub-auth` (06 §2.5 defines none); the stub is test wiring only. Adding such a name is the env schema's business (P1-FIX's file), not this unit's.
  - **events → `dbEventLogStore(auth.data)`** — the `events` insert under the service role (07 §5.1 rule 5, named in `auth`'s README); `queryEvents` / `countByName` **fail closed** (`event-log-read-not-available`): 03 §1.4's `Query` surface has no predicate and no aggregate, so the admin reads belong over 02 §7's views — the same `Query`-has-no-keyed-read gap `1a` hit. Console sink in development only (03 §9.5).
  - **consent → `dbConsentStore(auth.data)`** — `consent_records` · `biometric_consent_records` · `legal_documents` in session scope (RLS-bounded reads, filtered in memory); **the cookie half fails closed** (`cookie-consent-not-available`: a visitor's current row is a keyed read under the service role, which the surface cannot express), so `hasMarketing` still refuses — 07 §2.9's safe side. **Two recorded gaps:** `consent_records` has no `purpose` column, so a row with no document pair (`vaccination-status`, ADR-103; an informed action without a document) cannot be read back as its purpose — skipped, `hasConsent` answers `false` (02 §4.1 owner); and `latestConsent` under an admin session is a scan of every row the admin policy widens (acceptable day one, not an index).
  - **rate limiter → NOT declared `"shared"`, deliberately.** `rate_limit_buckets` has no specification (07 §8 names it; 02 §4 / §6 never create it — S5 item 2), and declaring the memory store "shared" is exactly the failure `assertSharedStore` exists to catch. So `consume` still denies in any production-resolved environment (preview included) — nothing calls it yet — and the boot logs one `warn`. **Unblocks only with a migration that specifies the table** (an S5-style unit), then `configureRateLimiter(store, "shared")`.
  - **areas → `db-areas` / `stub-areas` per `AREAS_SOURCE`** (05 §3 rule 1). `src/boot/db-areas.ts` reads the 291-row table once per process through the port and serves it from the same in-memory engine `stub-areas` runs on; a failed load answers the closed values and retries.
  - **comms → `stub-email` / `resend` per `EMAIL_PROVIDER` + `null-sms`**; `resend` (not installed) is refused at boot with `ALERT_ENV_INVALID` and the seam stays closed. The store and the renderer are installed **fail-closed** with the reasons `comms/types.ts` reserves (`store-not-configured` · `renderer-not-configured`): no template file exists and the `email_logs` store needs a keyed read (`findLiveByDedupeKey` · `read`). A send fails with the honest cause, not `comms-not-configured`.
  - **scheduling → the in-memory stub outside production only**; production stays on the fail-closed default (an in-memory calendar on a serverless runtime drops every booking on a cold start and tells no one). Selected by the resolved environment.
  - **Still not wired, unchanged:** `configurePurchaseProvider` (F-c's edge-bundle reason); the stage-model slices and `admin-on-behalf` (their insides are Phase 1e's — `registerSlice` keeps its runtime home in `positions`, P1-FIX moved only the types).
  - **The 23 cron routes still answer `no-handler-registered`** (E1 pinned it). **A deploy of this trunk shows a wall of `ALERT_CRON_FAILED` until the Phase 1 handlers land — expected; do not page on it.**
  - **E1 finding 4 closed in passing:** the boot alert's env names are logged under `invalidEnvVars` (the `envNames` key matched the scrubber's `name(e?s)?` pattern and was redacted); pinned in `boot.test.ts`.
- **No rate limiting on the auth surfaces (07 §8 row 3 — login · forgot / reset · set-password).** S3b's carried warning is why: `rateLimiter.consume` denies in any production-resolved environment (Vercel preview included) until boot declares a **shared** backend, and the shared store is the S5 `rate_limit_buckets` table. Wiring `consume` now would deny every login on the first preview deploy. Supabase Auth's own limits stay on meanwhile (07 §8 row 3 names them as part of the control). **Land it with S5's table**, not before.
- **The anonymous passwordless catch (AC-X-14) is not built.** 01 §4d step 3 — a _signed-in_ account with no password → `/set-password` — **is** built and tested (`needsPasswordSetup`, the `GateSession` signal). The login-form half, where an anonymous visitor types a known passwordless email and must be routed to set-password rather than shown an error, needs (a) a way to learn that an email exists with no password, which 02 §7 defines no RPC for and `auth.users` does not expose to `anon`, and (b) a ruling on the account-enumeration trade-off that answer creates (07 §4), plus the row-3 rate limit above. Raised as a foundations question rather than invented; it belongs with the S-X-08 / S-X-09 Rejig in Phase 1.
- **`signUp` cannot create the `user_profiles` row 02 §4.1 requires.** 02 §4.1 says the signup action writes "exactly one `user_roles` + one `user_profiles` row per user", but 03 §1.4's `SignUpInput` carries only email, password and role — no first name, last name, mobile or district. The connector writes `user_roles` (from a server value, never `admin`) and stops. Either `SignUpInput` widens, or `onboarding-parent` / `onboarding-nanny` own the profile row and 02 §4.1's wording moves with it. A foundations question for the planner; nothing is broken today because the table does not exist until `0002`.
- **`03 §11 row 9`'s grep gate is not yet green: 40 legacy files still import `@supabase/*`.** The rule is "no `@supabase/*` import outside `auth/` and `vetting-providers/providers/*`". S4 removed two (`src/lib/supabase/middleware.ts` + its spec, dead once the gate moved) and added none outside `src/modules/auth/lib/`. The remaining 40 are all in the legacy tree (`src/lib/supabase/{client,server,admin,storage}.ts` and their callers) and go with F-c / F-d.
- **★ BLOCKING DECISION — 01 §2.4 and 01 §2.3 contradict each other on whether a service module may import `platform`, and the contradiction is now measured, not theoretical.** S6's generated rule (on `main` since `0e8a75e`) forbids it; `npm run lint:boundaries` on the S4 branch reports **20 errors, every one `auth → @/modules/platform`** (the five `bb/no-relative-module-escape` hits the same run found in S4's own test files were S4's to fix, and are fixed: the specs now enter the module through `@/modules/auth` like any other caller). The two texts:
  - 01 §2.4: "Service modules stay leaves themselves (they import only `config` + `shared-types`; `auth ──► comms` removed — fix: A-7), so no cycle is possible."
  - 01 §2.3 footer: "(S) = service module, §2.4. The four service modules are importable by **every** module (ADR-069) **and the generated ESLint rule allows them for every row**".
    **The §2.4 reading is not satisfiable.** `auth` needs three things from `platform` and there is nowhere else to get them: `ok` / `err` / `fromThrown` (03 §1 rule 4 makes `Result` mandatory on every connector method, and `shared-types` is types-only by its own README — it holds the _type_, not the constructors), and `log` (01 §4b is the one logger, and 07 §10.1 requires `grantRole` to be logged, plus every fail-closed line the S4 silent-failure review added). Duplicating the `Result` constructors inside `auth` would give the app two of them — precisely the drift L1 and L3 exist to prevent. `platform` imports nothing, so **no cycle is possible either way**; §2.4's stated rationale ("so no cycle is possible") is satisfied by the §2.3 reading too.
    **What the planner has to rule:** (a) amend 01 §2.4 to say service modules may import `platform` and nothing else business-side — S6's generator then adds `platform` to the four service rows, one line in `scripts/boundaries/allowed-imports.ts`; or (b) hold §2.4 literally and move `ok` · `err` · `fromThrown` into `shared-types` and give `auth` its own logger seam — a change to two shared modules and a mechanical but wider edit. **S4 recommends (a).** Until it is ruled, `lint:boundaries` is red on PR #7 for this reason alone; S4 changed no lint file (S6's touch surface), as agreed in `BRANCHES.md`.

- **`eslint.legacy-paths.json` still lists `src/middleware.ts`.** It is no longer legacy — S4 rewrote it against the module tree (one connector import, no `process.env`, no literals) — so the boundary lint currently skips the gate. **S6 should drop that row** (the file is S6's; S4 did not touch it). `literal-exclusions.json` also still lists it, harmlessly: the new middleware passes `check:config-literals` either way, and that file is emptied by F-d.
- **The set-password destination is a third URL under screen S-X-09.** 01 §4d lists "set-password (ADR-042)" as a member of the `(auth)` group; 04 §6 gives S-X-09 as `/forgot-password` · `/reset-password` with a "set your password" **variant**. S4 built `/set-password` as its own route because the gate needs one stable, loop-free destination, and a screen ID already spans two URLs. If the planner would rather it be `/reset-password?mode=set`, it is a one-line change in `auth/lib/route-map.ts` plus moving the page.

The package is pinned exactly and wired in `instrumentation.ts` when BAI creates the project — one file, no connector change.
- **`platform/events/client.ts` (`track`) is not built** — it needs `POST /api/events` (F-c); `isClientEventName` + `validateEmitInput(input, 'client')` are the allow-list the route will call. The `vercel-analytics` and `meta` sinks are Phase 4c; the `Sink` type + `subscribe` registry are ready.

- **★ S3 security review — CLOSED by unit S3b (2026-09-15; branch `platform-security-150926-1`).** All five findings fixed, each with a failing test written first. **HIGH-1 `msg` was never scrubbed** → `create-logger.ts`'s `buildLine` now routes `msg` through `log/lib/scrub-message.ts` before the line is frozen, so the sink _and_ the tracker (`sentryErrorTracker` sends `line.msg` as the capture message) see the redacted form. A message is prose, not a value, so the whole-value predicate could not be applied directly: bearer pairs first (`Bearer <token>` spans two words), then each whitespace token through `looksLikePii`, then any spaced phone run, then `scrubString` as the backstop. Pinned at every level for an email · spaced phone · JWT · provider key · bearer token, plus the tracker seeing the scrubbed string. **HIGH-2 `SENSITIVE_KEY` missed plurals and three documented fields** → the boundary is now `(e?s)?($|_)` (`s?` alone still missed `addresses`), and `contact_number` · `user_agent` · `session_id` · bare `ip` were added; a `ConsentContext` passed as log fields now leaks none of its three values, pinned by a test that names `consent/types.ts`'s claim, and a second test pins the safe keys (`requestId`, `eventName`, `templateId`, `idempotencyKey`, `module`, `action`, `bucketKey`, `sinkId`) as _not_ collateral. **MEDIUM (all three fixed):** numbers and bigints are content-checked like strings, so a phone logged as a number under a benign key is redacted (`durationMs: 3`, `count: 1234`, an 8-digit stamp all survive); `toClientError` runs the new `lib/safe-details.ts` over `details` for **every** code, replacing `Error` instances and PII-shaped strings with `[redacted]` — one chokepoint, so `envelopeOf` and `toActionResult` inherit it, and it deliberately does not redact by key (a client `details` legitimately names its field — 01 §4c) nor touch numbers (the caller's own data); `rateLimiter.consume` now calls `assertSharedStore`, which in production denies (`INTERNAL`) and raises `ALERT_ENV_INVALID` while the registry still holds the per-instance memory limiter, instead of silently limiting per Vercel instance. Three helpers were extracted so the rules cannot drift: `lib/redaction-marks.ts`, `lib/phone-like-runs.ts` (one definition of the phone shape, shared by the predicate and the substitution) and `log/lib/scrub-string.ts`.
  - **Known trade-off, deliberate and documented (module README):** `msg` is fail-closed, so a 9–15-digit run inside prose reads as a phone number — an epoch stamp, a date written `2026-09-15 08`, the numeric tail of a hex-lettered uuid — and is redacted _in place_ (the rest of the message survives). **Identifiers belong in `fields`, not in the message** (01 §4b); `ts`, `userId` and `requestId` are fields for exactly this reason.
  - **What the three review agents then found on top (all closed in the same unit).** `safeDetails` was running `looksLikePii` over whole values, and that predicate anchors JWT / provider key / bearer at the **start** of a string — so a key pasted mid-sentence in a provider's message would have shipped to the browser (silent-failure-hunter, CRITICAL). The token-wise pass was extracted to `lib/scrub-free-text.ts` and applied at **all three prose boundaries**: `scrubMessage`, `safeDetails`, and `events/lib/pii-safe-string.ts` — which had the same gap and now refuses any value the scrubber would change (a `label` or `landingPath` carrying an embedded token used to pass validation into the event pipeline). `assertSharedStore` compared limiter _identity_, so a second memory-backed limiter installed at boot passed the guard — and the branch's own test asserted exactly that while claiming the opposite (code-reviewer, HIGH); it now keys on a **declared** backend, `configureRateLimiter(limiter, backend)` defaulting to `"memory"` so a boot that never says keeps failing closed. `safeDetails` redacted by value only, so an `apiKey` with an ordinary-looking value crossed to the client (code-reviewer, HIGH); `lib/key-patterns.ts` now splits the vocabulary into **`secret`** (credentials and raw payloads — never to a client) and **`personal`** (the requester's own data, legitimately _named_ in a validation `details` per 01 §4c), the log scrubber redacting both and `safeDetails` the secret class only. The `err("INTERNAL", MESSAGE, undefined, { reason })` call put the reason in `cause`, and an unsound `as Result<…>` hid it (typescript-reviewer, HIGH) — now `err<RateLimitDetails>("INTERNAL", MESSAGE)` with no cast, the reason in the `ALERT_ENV_INVALID` log line where an operator reads it. MEDIUMs: `safeValue` flattened a `Date` / `Map` / class instance to `{}` (a silent loss) → non-plain objects are redacted whole; numeric over-redaction → `lib/phone-like-number.ts` exempts the millisecond-epoch band so timestamps stay readable, and the same check now runs at _both_ boundaries, which had been inconsistent; `buildLine` ran two regex pipelines over caller-supplied text **outside** the logger's try/catch → wrapped, with a reported placeholder line, so a failing scrub can never reach business code.
  - **Then the `security-reviewer` re-review of the two HIGHs: both CLOSED**, verified adversarially rather than by reading (`msg` scrubbed on every path to a sink and to the tracker, `child()` included; every plural form and all three consent fields redacted flat and nested, with the safe keys still readable). It found **one new HIGH and one MEDIUM, both fixed in the same unit.** HIGH: `looksLikePii`'s `EMAIL` is unanchored with two greedy quantifiers, so a long string holding an `@` but no valid address makes the engine retry at every offset — **measured 3.3 s for an 80 kB value, against 0 ms bounded**. S3b is what made that reachable on unbounded input: the scrubber ran over the whole `msg` before any truncation, and over every string in `details` with no cap at all, so one logged provider body could hold the Node event loop and deny every concurrent request on the instance. `scrubFreeText` now cuts to `MAX_SCANNED` **before** any pattern touches the text — the shape `events/lib/pii-safe-string.ts` already had for free via zod's `.max(256)` — and `looksLikePii` pre-filters `EMAIL` behind a cheap `includes("@")`. `log/lib/scrub-message.ts` folded away in the process: with the bound inside `scrubFreeText`, `scrubString`'s whole-value backstop added nothing the token pass had not already caught. MEDIUM: `KEY_PATTERNS.secret`'s `document` prefix swallowed **`documentId` and `documentVersion` — `consent/types.ts`'s own `DocumentVersion`, the audit signal of a consent event, not document contents** — plus `cookieConsentId` and `consentRecordId`; all four are now on the safe list explicitly, _not_ as a blanket `*_id` exemption, because `sessionId` must stay redacted. LOW: `normaliseKey` could not split an acronym run, so `IPAddress` escaped redaction while `ipAddress` did not, and `telephone` was not word-bounded — both closed.
  - **Two guards were mutation-checked** (removing the code fails the test): the bound inside `scrubFreeText`, and the production wiring in `default-rate-limiter.ts` — the latter because its first version could not fail (`NODE_ENV` is `test` for the whole suite), so it was rewritten to run on a fresh module graph with `@/modules/config` stubbed.
  - **Recorded, not fixed — `NODE_ENV === "production"` also covers Vercel preview.** It is the only production signal a client-safe leaf can read (`resolveEnvironment` is `server-only`), and firing on preview is deliberate: a preview that shares a database should share a rate-limit store, and a guard first exercised in production is not a guard. The consequence to know: **the first route wired to `rateLimiter.consume` will deny on preview until boot calls `configureRateLimiter(limiter, "shared")`** — intended, but S4 must land that wiring before anything calls `consume`. A true `VERCEL_ENV`-backed client-safe flag would be an S2 env-registry change (a foundations edit), out of S3b's scope.
  - **Carried forward to S6 — now CLOSED.** The finding also proposed _a lint rule against template-interpolated `msg`_. Landed as **`bb/no-interpolated-log-message`** in `eslint.boundaries.js` (S6): a call whose receiver chain roots at `log` / `logger` may not take a template literal carrying `${...}` as its message; `log.child({ … }).warn(…)` counts, a template with no expressions passes. Zero violations in `src/modules/**` today — it is a guard for what S4 and the fan-out lanes add. The runtime scrubber remains the thing that makes the leak impossible; this is the second line.

- **`public/katie-manifest.json` is stale on `main` beyond its timestamp.** Re-running `prebuild` (which every `next build` does) regenerates it with `cron_count` **23**, not the committed 13 — S2 regenerated `vercel.json` from `config/crons.ts` but the manifest was not re-stamped with it, so the committed file still lists the 13 kept Sydney crons. Found by S3b while measuring the `build` gate; **reverted, not committed** (outside S3b's touch surface, and the known-bug note above says not to commit manifest churn). Whoever owns the manifest next (S6 or F-c, per the "generate at build only and ignore the file" candidate above) should regenerate it once deliberately rather than let a build do it by accident.

- **CI workflow prints its placeholder env values in every step's `env:` block** (`STRIPE_SECRET_KEY: sk_test_ci-dummy`, `CRON_SECRET: placeholder-cron-secret`, seen on PR #1). Harmless while they are dummies, but a literal in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is never masked by GitHub. **The first unit that needs a real credential in CI must take it from a GitHub Actions secret, not a literal**, or it will be printed in the run log. Recorded on PR #1.
- **Measured CI on PR #1 (2026-09-15) — `typecheck` passes, seven red, each with a named fixer.** `lint` prettier over the legacy tree (S1) · `banned-literals` legacy literals (S1 / S2) · `allowed-imports` generator absent (S6) · `types-drift` no generated DB types (S2 / S5) · `test` three specs assert the stripped N-2 payouts cron (S1) · `build` `size-limit` is not a dependency (Phase 1 / F-c) · `gitleaks` **the secret scan is clean — `no leaks found`** — the job fails on its `check:pins` step over the raw copy's `^` ranges (S1). `HANDOFF.md` §11 row S0 carries the same table.

## Files created / modified in the previous unit (S3)

Commits on `platform-150926-1`, oldest first (`git log dd7cd7f..HEAD`): `feat(s3): platform connector` (index.ts + types.ts, reviewed before any inside — L2) · `test(s3): platform suites` (RED) · `feat(s3): platform insides` · `docs(s3): platform README` · review fixes · this ledger update. No package added, no config / shared-types / script / workflow change.

**Design decisions recorded in the L-005 S3 entry:** boot-time `configure*` registries (fail closed until wired) instead of importing server env in the module — `platform` stays client-safe and a leaf; `EventEnvelope` / `EmitInput` distributive over `EventName` so `props` narrows with `name`; the `EventPropsMap` augmentation targets `@/modules/shared-types/events` (S6 lint allowance); no `@sentry/nextjs` yet (injected `ErrorCaptureFn`); `connection.origin` / `signupSource` props follow 03 §9.3's unions, not 02's enums (foundations gap raised); `ConsentParty` derived locally because the shared-types barrel does not export `CustomerRole` (S2 gap).

**Design decisions recorded in the L-005 S2 entry:** two config connectors (`@/modules/config` client-safe, `@/modules/config/server` behind `server-only`) and two `process.env` readers (`env.ts` all 50 names / `public-env.ts` the 13 public names as literal accesses) — forced by 01 §3.3's `server-only` guard + 07 §7 item 3's bundle scan; the env registry split into a public table and a server table for the same reason; `VERCEL_ENV` selects the 06 §2.5 column, `NODE_ENV=production` at runtime fails closed as production, a `next build` (NEXT_PHASE) validates as development; the BUNDLE price-id pair is not defined (03 §5.2 owns the ids) so the registry has **50** names, not HANDOFF's 51.

## Files created / modified in the previous unit (S3b)

Commits on `platform-security-150926-1`, oldest first (`git log b0930f8..HEAD`): `test(s3b)` (RED — 13 failing, 28 passing) · `fix(s3b)` (GREEN) · `docs(s3b)` ledgers · `fix(s3b)` review round · `fix(s3b)` security re-review round · this ledger update. (The unpushed tail was rewritten once: a `git add -A` had swept a probe file a running review agent wrote into `__tests__/` into a ledger commit, and its JWT-shaped fixture tripped `gitleaks` in git mode — removing it in a later commit would not have cleared the history, so the two commits after it were rebuilt without it. No probe file is in any commit on this branch.) **Created (11):** `platform/lib/{redaction-marks,phone-like-runs,phone-like-number,normalise-key,key-patterns,scrub-free-text,safe-details}.ts` · `platform/log/lib/scrub-string.ts` · `platform/rate-limit/lib/{memory-backed-rate-limiter,assert-shared-store,rate-limit-backend-registry}.ts`. **Modified (13):** `platform/lib/{looks-like-pii,to-client-error,envelope-of,normalise-key,key-patterns,scrub-free-text}.ts` · `platform/log/lib/{scrub-pii,create-logger}.ts` · `platform/events/lib/pii-safe-string.ts` · `platform/rate-limit/{types.ts, lib/rate-limiter-registry.ts, lib/default-rate-limiter.ts, lib/configure-rate-limiter.ts}` · `platform/README.md` · the four suites `platform.{log,result,rate-limit,events}.test.ts`. **Modified outside the module:** `docs/build-progress.md` · `CHANGELOG.md` only. No package, config, shared-types, script or workflow change; no module other than `platform` touched.

**Deviation from the declared touch surface, cited:** the brief scoped S3b to `platform/log/**`, `platform/lib/to-client-error.ts` and `platform/rate-limit/**`. Three of the new files sit in `platform/lib/` beside `to-client-error.ts` rather than inside it — forced by L1 (one export per file): `safe-details.ts` is `to-client-error`'s helper, and `redaction-marks.ts` + `phone-like-runs.ts` exist so the log scrubber and the client-error guard share one definition of the marks and of the phone shape instead of duplicating regexes. `looks-like-pii.ts` was edited only to consume `phone-like-runs.ts`; its behaviour is unchanged and the events module's `piiSafeString`, which depends on it, is unaffected (its suite passes untouched).

**Design decisions recorded in the L-005 S3b entry:** `msg` is scrubbed token-wise rather than whole-value (a message is prose; the whole-value predicate would either miss embedded provider keys or nuke every message containing a date); `safeDetails` redacts by value only, never by key, because a client `details` legitimately names its own field (01 §4c); the rate-limit assertion keys on `publicEnv.NODE_ENV === "production"` — the only production signal available to a client-safe leaf — which also fires on Vercel preview, deliberately (preview should have a shared store too); `assertSharedStore` takes the limiter and the flag as parameters rather than reading the registry, so it is testable without environment stubbing and is not part of the connector surface.

**Created (50):** everything under `src/modules/auth/**` (registry above) · `src/app/(auth)/set-password/page.tsx`. **Modified outside the module (3):** `src/middleware.ts` (rewritten as the 01 §4d gate) · `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` (one line — it read only `?redirect=`, so the gate's `next=` was silently dropped after sign-in) · `src/app/admin/katie-stats/page.tsx` (a comment pointing at the gate this unit deleted). **Deleted (2):** `src/lib/supabase/middleware.ts` + `middleware.test.ts` — the Sydney gate, dead once `src/middleware.ts` stopped calling `updateSession`; nothing else imported them, and leaving a second, weaker gate in the tree is something a later unit re-wires by accident. **No package added; no `config`, `shared-types`, `platform`, script, workflow or eslint file touched.** `public/katie-manifest.json` regenerated by a `prebuild` during a local `next build` and was **reverted, not committed** (the standing rule above).

**Design decisions recorded in the L-005 S4 entry:** the `AuthDriver` seam (one narrow interface over `@supabase/ssr`, so no `@supabase/*` type crosses the connector and `stub-auth` swaps the **driver**, not the contract behaviour — the gate, the `Result` mapping and the password policy stay the module's own, which is what stops a stub drifting from the real thing); `needsPasswordSetup()` as a flagged connector extension and `GateSession` as a widened `refreshSession` return, both because 01 §4d step 3 needs a signal 03 §1.4 does not name and the edge has no second round-trip; the module binding defaults to the **real** inside rather than failing closed (the opposite of `platform`, for the reason recorded in the module README); `/set-password` as its own route under screen S-X-09; an `aal1` admin is refused from `/admin/*` but left alone inside the `(auth)` group, so the missing enrol screen cannot become a redirect loop; a signed-in principal with no `user_roles` row is **not** a session (fail closed) and is logged as a provisioning anomaly; `SignUpInput.role` is a `CustomerRole`, derived locally because the `shared-types` barrel still does not export it (the same S2 gap `platform` hit for `ConsentParty`).

## Files created / modified in the current unit (S6 — module-boundary lint)

Branch `boundary-lint-160926-1`, pushed, **PR [#6](https://github.com/babybloomsydney/BBLDN/pull/6)** open into `main` — **not merged** (needs BAI's OK for that merge). Commits, oldest first (`git log 04569f8..HEAD`): `test(s6)` suites (RED — 7 files fail to collect, no tests run) · `feat(s6)` generator + generated config (GREEN) · `ci(s6)` the `allowed-imports` job · `feat(s6)` `bb/no-interpolated-log-message` · `chore(s6)` explicit `@typescript-eslint` deps · `feat(s6)` `bb/no-dynamic-module-import` · `fix(s6)` review round (code-reviewer + silent-failure-hunter) · `test(s6)` branch coverage · `fix(s6)` typescript-reviewer round · this ledger update.

**Created (18):** `scripts/gen-boundary-rules.ts` · `scripts/boundaries/{allowed-imports,service-modules,universal-modules,extra-entry-points}.ts` · `scripts/boundaries/lib/{allowed-specifiers,restricted-import-group,assert-acyclic,parse-architecture-table,architecture-parity,check-result,render-boundary-config}.ts` · `scripts/boundaries/rules/{index,one-export-per-file,no-relative-module-escape,no-interpolated-log-message,no-dynamic-module-import}.js` · `eslint.boundaries.js` (generated) · `scripts/boundaries/__tests__/` (10 suites, 105 tests).
**Modified:** `package.json` (`gen:boundary-rules` · `check:allowed-imports` · `lint:boundaries`; `gates` now chains both; `@types/eslint` + `@typescript-eslint/parser` + `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin` pinned exactly) · `package-lock.json` · `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `allowed-imports` job only) · `.prettierignore` (the generated config) · `vitest.config.ts` (`include` gains `scripts/**`) · `docs/build-progress.md` · `CHANGELOG.md`. **No file under `src/modules/**` was touched\*\* — which is what makes S6 and S4 safe in parallel.

**How the table reaches the generator.** `01-architecture.md` §2.3 is the authority, but the foundations live in the sibling `LDN/` tree (ADR-107 "linked") and are **not in the CI checkout**, so a generator that parsed the markdown could not run in the `allowed-imports` job at all. `scripts/boundaries/allowed-imports.ts` is the machine-readable copy — the one place the table is written twice — and two things keep it honest: a vitest suite that parses the real document and compares row by row, and `check:allowed-imports` itself, which runs the same parity check and **prints the result in the job log every run** (OK with the row count where the sibling tree is present; an explicit NOTICE naming the gap where it is not — `scripts/ci/check-claude-md.sh`'s precedent).

**What the generated config enforces** (flat config; ESLint 8.57 needs `ESLINT_USE_FLAT_CONFIG=true`, which `npm run lint:boundaries` carries): 05 §7 **rule 1** — one `no-restricted-imports` block per module, the whole `@/modules` namespace banned and only the row's connectors negated back (gitignore semantics, last match wins); **rule 2** — deep imports die with the namespace ban, `@/modules/config/server` is negated as `config`'s second entry point (01 §3.3; S2's requirement), and sub-module connectors (`comms/sms`, `admin/*` …) are reachable only through the parent; **rule 3** — module-level acyclicity asserted over the declared table at generation time, which is exhaustive once rules 1–2 hold; **rule 4** — `bb/one-export-per-file`. Plus three guards for what ESLint 8's core rule cannot see. The legacy tree is excluded by reading `eslint.legacy-paths.json` at run time, never duplicating it into the artefact.

**Design decisions recorded in the L-005 S6 entry:** flat config (the only way to register a rule under the exact id 05 §7 rule 4 names); the machine-readable copy + the two parity gates; type-only exports counted as L1's "one type group"; `__tests__/**` read as the folder form of the spec's `*.test.ts` exemption for the one-export rule; `import/no-cycle` **not** used because it silently no-ops under flat config on this ESLint version (measured), acyclicity asserted at generation instead.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (F-c — payments · purchase-paths · access-gate · app · admin connectors + the route shells)

Branch `modules-payments-160926-1`, off `origin/main` `ad059d0`. **ADR-117 Tier A — the review battery ran INLINE**, not in the overnight sweep: this unit touches money, access control and public entry points, which is exactly what the tier exists to protect.

**Created (94): 70 under `src/modules/{payments,purchase-paths,access-gate,app,admin}/**`, 24 under `src/app/**`.** Modified 17, deleted 2.

- **`purchase-paths`** — the swappable provider seam (03 §5.2 / §5.5). Connector + types + fail-closed registry; `providers/stub-stripe/` behind the **three guards of 07 §5.5 / ADR-108**: it is never exported from the module's `index.ts` (the only path to it is a guarded import at boot, so no connector import can pull stub code into a bundle — `check:bundle-secrets` is the acceptance), `assertStubAllowed` **throws on construction** in a production-resolved environment, and `parseEvent` fails closed on a **constant-time** `STUB_EVENT_SECRET` compare plus a zod schema (`stub-event-schema.ts`) that caps every id and refuses a non-`GBP` currency or a negative amount. **There is no `stripe-uk` provider** — no Stripe SDK call, no key, no payout logic (N-2, ADR-022); with `PURCHASE_PROVIDER = stripe-uk` the registry stays unconfigured and every call fails closed.
- **`payments`** — the app's one view of money and access. `PurchasePath` (03 §5.2 in full: both paths, the spine, `getAccess`, `startTrial`, `openDfyAccess`, `setAccess`, `portal`, `prices`), the `AccessState` union, the 03 §5.3 `E_*` reason vocabulary, a fail-closed registry and `stubPayments`. The stub implements **only rules the foundations state** — the admin toggle and its two events (§5.4.6), `E_DFY_FAMILY` (ADR-093), `openDfyAccess`'s `paymentDueAt` and satisfaction window (ADR-094), `E_PAYMENT_NOT_DUE`, and the presets from `PRICES`. The §5.4.3 dispatch table is **not** stubbed: a verified event it cannot place is `ignored`, never a guessed money transition.
- **`access-gate`** — `decideAccess`, the pure gate rule, over `payments.getAccess`; `setAccess` is a straight re-export so exactly one place can flip a family's access. Every branch is quoted from 03 §5.2 / §5.4.5 / §5.4.6: the admin toggle overrides both ways, `deposit-paid` opens **nothing** (ADR-097), `placed` is open before any bill (ADR-093), and `accessUntil` closes the gate once passed without waiting for the lapse cron.
- **`app`** — `katie` · `child-development` · `child-linking` sub-folders with their own `index.ts`; the one runtime surface is `childLinking`'s fail-closed reads, because the youngest linked child's date of birth is what bounds a family's access (ADR-083 / 084). Katie and child-development ship **types only**: no section states their connector signatures.
- **`admin`** — the **eight** panels (`call-queue` · `calendar` · `positions-panel` · `pipeline` · `leads` · `users` · `support` · `guarantees`; ADR-074 and ADR-088 made it eight), each a folder with its own `index.ts` and descriptor, and `ADMIN_PANELS` assembled from the eight so a folder and the nav set cannot disagree. Routes and screen IDs are quoted from 04 §6.4.
- **Route shells.** `src/app/api/_lib/` (`authorise-bearer` · `constant-time-equals` · `request-id` · `cron-spec-for` · `run-cron` · `refuse-cron`); **23 cron shells generated one-for-one from `config/crons.ts`** (13 legacy Sydney routes replaced, 10 new); `/api/webhooks/stripe` (signature required before parse, the legacy Sydney handler set removed); `/api/agent` (Bearer `ADMIN_API_TOKEN`); `/api/dev/stub-purchase` (404 outside development / preview, then `requireRole('admin')` with aal2, then the shared secret); `/api/health` (deliberately the least informative route in the app); `/admin/calls` + `/admin/guarantees` page shells, both role-gated in the page.
- **`src/instrumentation.ts`** (+ `next.config.mjs` `instrumentationHook`) — see the ★ entry above. It makes `check:prod-guard` green for the first time.
- **Operational note (code-reviewer LOW).** Every one of the 23 cron shells, `/api/agent` and `/api/admin/pipeline-snapshots` now answers **500 `no-handler-registered`** once the caller is authorised. That is the point — a 200 would read to an operator as "the job ran" — but whoever first deploys this branch should expect a wall of cron-failure alerts and not page on them. Nothing is deployed today.
- **`public/katie-manifest.json`** regenerated **deliberately**, as the known bug asked whoever owned it next to do: `cron_count` 13 → 23, `route_count` 48 → 61, `page_count` 84 → 87. It now matches the tree.

**ADR-117 Tier A — the three reviews ran inline; verdicts and what changed.**

| Review                | CRITICAL | HIGH | MEDIUM | LOW | Verdict                                     |
| --------------------- | -------- | ---- | ------ | --- | ------------------------------------------- |
| `security-reviewer`   | 0        | 0    | 1      | 4   | no CRITICAL/HIGH; the MEDIUM closed in-unit |
| `code-reviewer`       | 0        | 2    | 4      | 3   | both HIGH closed in-unit                    |
| `typescript-reviewer` | —        | —    | —      | —   | see the L-005 F-c PROGRESS entry            |

- **Closed in-unit (security).** `stub-stripe.parseEvent` compared the presented signature against the injected secret with no empty-secret guard — and `constantTimeEquals("", "")` is `true`. Not reachable through either caller today (the webhook route rejects an empty `stripe-signature` first, and `/api/dev/stub-purchase` has already proved the secret non-empty), but it broke 07 §5.5's "each layer sufficient **alone**" claim. A `verified()` guard now applies the same rule `authoriseBearer` does, pinned by a case that builds the provider with a blank secret and presents a blank one. Also bounded `instalments.count` / `index` / `paidCount` in the boundary schema, and `ALERT_ENV_INVALID` now carries the failing env **names** (never values) so an on-call engineer can act on it.
- **Closed in-unit (code).** `stubPayments` was one 152-line function — split into four method groups, nothing over 50 lines. The stub's "refuse to guess" webhook outcome was **untested**: a regression that reported `handled` and moved a family's access would have passed every other case; now pinned, and mutation-checked (flipping `ignored` → `handled` fails the suite). `decideAccess` gained a `never`-typed exhaustiveness arm so a ninth `AccessState` variant is a compile error rather than a silent close. Added the `until === now` boundary case. Fixed a comment naming a loader file that does not exist, and renamed `earliest` → `youngestDateOfBirth` (it returns the maximum date of birth).
- **Closed in-unit (typescript).** Both HIGHs were the two already fixed above — the `as never` casts that `stubPayments`' refactor removed, and `decideAccess`'s non-exhaustive `default:` — and the reviewer verified each against the committed snapshot (it added a ninth `AccessStanding` variant and measured that `tsc` stayed silent; it also measured that `x as never` accepts anything, which `as CustomerRef` does not). The four remaining `as never` casts in this unit's **tests** are now real brands. `parsed.data as unknown as PurchaseEvent` is gone entirely: `stub-event-schema.ts` brands `ref` and `at` at the boundary that validated them, so the schema's output type **is** `PurchaseEvent` and a drift between the two is now a compile error rather than something the `unknown` hop absorbed. The nested `AccessState` objects in `toggledChange` / `openDfyAccess` / `startTrial` are frozen before they are aliased into the stub's mutable map (`readonly` is a compile-time promise only). `AccessGateErrorDetails.reason` was a bare `string` while every sibling was a closed union — it is now `PaymentsErrorReason`. The `LOCALE.currency as Money["currency"]` cast was a no-op and is gone, so a currency drift between `config` and the contract is a compile error. The reviewer also confirmed the "declared, then frozen" pattern is genuinely enforced (removing a binding's annotation produces ~15 `TS7006`s), that intersecting `AccessStanding` with `{ deposit? }` does not break discrimination, and that no route handler has a floating promise.
- **Observed, not this unit's to fix:** ten `as never` brand casts remain in F-a's and F-b's test files (`matching`, `placements`, `call-layer`, `scheduling`, `admin-on-behalf`, `connections`, `positions`, `auth`). `x as never` accepts any value, so those mocks do not enforce their own contracts. One line each for the Tier B fix unit.
- **Recorded, not fixed — each outside this unit's touch surface.** (i) 07 §5.5 item 2 also requires `STUB_EVENT_SECRET` to differ from every other secret; `config/lib/refine-env.ts` has no cross-secret check (S2's file). (ii) `constantTimeEquals` exists twice — route layer and `stub-stripe` — because a route may not deep-import a module's `lib/` and a module may not import from `src/app/`; it belongs in `platform`, which this unit does not touch. (iii) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` still labels the `prod-guard` step "red until F-c"; it is green now, but CI is not this unit's surface.
- **Mutation-checked guards** (removing the code fails a test): `authoriseBearer`'s absent-secret rejection, and `stubPayments.handleWebhook`'s `ignored` outcome.

**Gates measured locally on this branch:** `typecheck` · `lint` · `prettier --check .` · `vitest run` **2 472 / 2 472 (171 files)** · `lint:boundaries` · `check:allowed-imports` · `check:config-literals` · `check:env-reads` · `check:claude-md` · `crons:check` (23) · `env:check` · `build` · **`check:prod-guard` (green for the first time)** · **`check:bundle-secrets`** (175 client chunks, no server-only name, no `stub-stripe`).

**One measured finding worth carrying:** the `build` job now needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` in its environment. It always did — `parse-public-env.ts` requires the **preview** column whenever `NODE_ENV = production` — but until F-c no `src/app/**` route imported `@/modules/config`, so page-data collection never parsed the public env. CI already sets it (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`); a local `next build` needs it too.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (S5 — the migration set)

**Created (28).** `supabase/config.toml` · `supabase/.gitignore` · **`supabase/migrations/0000`–`0016`** (17 files, 8 130 lines, of which ~500 are the two generated seeds) · **`supabase/rollbacks/0000`–`0016`** (17 twins — see the deviation below on why they are not in `migrations/`) · `supabase/__tests__/db-client.ts` · `constraints.test.ts` (`db.constraints`) · `enum-ordinals.test.ts` · `rls-fixtures.ts` · `rls.test.ts` (`int.rls`) · **`src/modules/shared-types/database.types.ts`** (5 045 lines, generated, never hand-edited).

**Modified (8).** `src/modules/shared-types/types.ts` (re-exports `Database` / `Tables` / `TablesInsert` / `TablesUpdate` / `Enums`) · `src/modules/shared-types/README.md` · `src/modules/auth/types.ts` (**the one-line `AppDatabase` swap** S4 named) · `src/modules/auth/lib/supabase-query.ts` (re-audited: the unwrap helpers are generic in the row type, so the one unavoidable driver-payload assertion lives in one documented place instead of nine) · `src/modules/auth/lib/memory-auth-driver.ts` (same seam in the stub) · `src/modules/auth/__tests__/auth.binding.test.ts` (a `user_roles` fixture that wrote a row with no `user_id` — the swap found it) · `vitest.config.ts` (two projects: `unit` and `integration`) · `package.json` (`test` / `test:coverage` name `--project unit`; new `test:db` and `types:generate`; `@types/pg` pinned).

**Deviations, both deliberate and recorded as `[decision]` in the L-005 S5 entry.** (1) **One PR, not seventeen** — HANDOFF §11 says one PR per migration; that is seventeen CI runs for one coherent schema, so this is one branch, **one commit per migration**, one PR, with the `database-reviewer` findings recorded per migration in the PR body. (2) **The rollback twins live in `supabase/rollbacks/`, not beside the forward files.** 06 §4.2 names `supabase/migrations/NNNN_<group>.rollback.sql`, but the Supabase CLI — which the same section mandates for applying — treats any `NNNN_*.sql` in `migrations/` as a migration and **would apply the rollbacks**, measured on a dry run. The twin keeps the same basename one directory across.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (E1 — the shell smoke)

Branch `shell-smoke-160926-1`, off `origin/main` `1ec71e1`. **ADR-117 Tier A under ADR-120** — it asserts the behaviour of the auth gate, the cron / webhook / agent shells and the admin routes, so the review battery ran **inline**. **It changes no `src/**`file.** A smoke suite that asserts whatever the code happens to do is worthless, so every assertion is written from a`doc §` and the five places where the code and the foundations disagree are in Known bugs above, not bent into a passing test.

**Created (13).** `tests/e2e/shell-smoke/{envelope.ts,secrets.ts,boot-and-health.spec.ts,auth-gate.spec.ts,cron-shells.spec.ts,machine-routes.spec.ts,production/stub-refused.spec.ts}` · `playwright.shell-smoke.config.ts` · `scripts/ci/{boot-guard.sh,shell-smoke.sh,shell-smoke-server.sh}` · `scripts/ci/lib/{smoke-env.sh,boot-outcome.sh}`. **Modified (5).** `scripts/ci/prod-guard-boot.sh` · `playwright.config.ts` (one `testIgnore`) · `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the e2e stage) · `package.json` (`test:shell-smoke`, `check:boot-guard`) · this ledger.

- **Two servers, not one, both serving the same `next build` output.** `shell-smoke-server.sh preview` runs at `VERCEL_ENV=preview` (the ordinary shape: gate, crons, webhook, agent, admin); `… production` runs at `VERCEL_ENV=production` (07 §5.5 layer 3: the stub purchase route must not exist). Both are `next start`, never `next dev` — the thing E1 asserts is the **assembled** application, so a missing `.next/` is a loud failure rather than a dev-server fallback.
- **`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` points at a closed loopback port on purpose.** 01 §4d step 2 refuses a session it cannot read rather than waving it through — the half Sydney got wrong — so the smoke wants Supabase _unreachable_, not merely un-cookied. It also keeps the suite offline, deterministic and credential-free: no value in `smoke-env.sh` is a secret, and `bb-ldn-preview` was **not** written to (nor read) by this unit.
- **What the 79 Playwright assertions + 6 boot-guard cases actually prove.** The app boots and `/api/health` answers 200 · the boot **exits non-zero** on a missing secret, a malformed value and the dev-bypass name, and a valid env still listens (the positive control, without which every negative case could pass for the wrong reason) · seven protected paths under `/parent`, `/nanny`, `/admin` answer 307 to `/login?next=…` and the page body is never served · all five `(auth)` pages render · **all 23 declared cron routes** refuse an absent, blank and wrong bearer with a 401 envelope carrying a uuid `requestId` matching `x-request-id` · `/api/webhooks/stripe` refuses an unsigned and a blank-signature request and never answers 2xx to a forged one · `/api/agent` refuses all three bad-bearer shapes and its token is **not** accepted on an admin surface · `/api/admin/pipeline-snapshots` refuses an unauthenticated read and the cron bearer · in production the stub purchase route 404s before the secret is even read.
- **Why at the assembled level when unit suites exist.** `run-cron.test.ts` proves the helper, and separately proves a route _file_ exists per declared path. Neither proves a given route file is wired to the helper, or wired with the right path. Twenty-three routes are asserted through Next's own router because that is the only place that mistake shows.
- **The dev bypass is proved twice because it has two mechanisms** (01 §4d step 4; 07 §5.4 row 4): the registry marks `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEV_MODE` dev-only, so a non-development deployment that sets it **does not boot** (`boot-guard.sh` case 3); and this _is_ a production build, in which `PUBLIC_FLAGS.DEV_MODE` is statically false, so the gate still refuses (`auth-gate.spec.ts`).
- **Assertion 7 — the expected-but-noisy baseline.** An authorised cron answers **500 `INTERNAL`** (`no-handler-registered` server-side): F-c's deliberate choice, since a 200 would read to an operator as "the job ran". Pinned once per route so each Phase 1 handler flips exactly one assertion. **Whoever first deploys this trunk should expect a wall of `ALERT_CRON_FAILED` and not page on it.** The reason string is not on the wire — 01 §4a rule 1 keeps an `INTERNAL` message generic and `toClientError` strips `details` — so the wire evidence is the status and the code.
  **ADR-117 Tier A — the three reviews ran inline; verdicts and what changed.**

| Review                | CRITICAL | HIGH | MEDIUM | LOW | Verdict                                                           |
| --------------------- | -------- | ---- | ------ | --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `security-reviewer`   | 0        | 0    | 1      | 2   | approve; the MEDIUM and both LOW closed in-unit                   |
| `code-reviewer`       | 0        | 2    | 2      | 1   | both HIGH closed in-unit; one MEDIUM carried with its owner named |
| `typescript-reviewer` | 0        | 0    | 5      | 1   | approve; four of the five MEDIUM closed in-unit                   |

- **Closed in-unit (code, both HIGH).** (i) `boot-guard.sh` had **no cleanup trap** — it starts up to six `next start` children, and an interrupt between cases would orphan one holding a port for the life of the host; its sibling `prod-guard-boot.sh` had had that trap since F-c. (ii) The cause was the second HIGH: the start-and-judge loop was **hand-copied** in both scripts, which is exactly why the loopback bind landed in one and the trap in the other. Both are now `scripts/ci/lib/boot-outcome.sh`, one harness with the trap, the loopback bind and the grace period in it; `prod-guard-boot.sh` is a `case` over its result. `boot_outcome` **writes `BOOT_OUTCOME` rather than echoing** — a `$(…)` capture runs in a subshell, and the caller's trap could then never see the pid of the server it started, which is the bug in miniature.
- **Closed in-unit (security).** The one MEDIUM: the forged-signature assertion is currently true because `payments` is unconfigured, not because a signature was rejected — it would pass identically with verification deleted. Not hidden before, now **starred in the test with the owner named** (the payments-inside PR must tighten it to `422 / VALIDATION / E_EVENT_UNVERIFIED`) and recorded in Known bugs above. Both LOW closed: all three `next start` invocations bind `--hostname 127.0.0.1` (they stand up real auth logic, and nothing off this machine has business probing them), and `trace` is `"off"` rather than a dead `"on-first-retry"` under `retries: 0` — which also means no artefact ever holds a request header.
- **Closed in-unit (typescript).** The specs no longer restate the envelope inline: `envelope.ts` imports the **real** `ErrorEnvelope` / `ErrorStatus` from `platform`, so a rename of `error.code` or a change to the 01 §4a status table is a `tsc` failure at every call site rather than a red suite. `errorEnvelopeOf` **narrows instead of casting** and throws naming the route and the body's **keys** (never its values) — which also closed the reviewer's MEDIUM 1: `expect(body.data).toBeUndefined()` would have passed if the admin funnel leaked under any other key, whereas a guard requiring an `error` object means a response carrying `data` cannot reach an assertion at all. A port override is validated (`Number("")` is `0`, not `NaN` — the CI-shaped failure), and the `webServer` helper is annotated with Playwright's own type, derived because Playwright declares but does not export it.
- **Carried, with its owner named:** the placeholder environment is now a **third** hand-kept copy (`smoke-env.sh`, `.env.test`, the CI `env:` block). Generating it from `config/lib/env-schema.ts` is its own small unit — see Next unit — and was not folded into a smoke suite.
- **Mutation-checked, not assumed** (removing the code makes the guard fail): `prod-guard-boot.sh` reports FAIL the moment the stub provider and its secret are removed from its environment — so the non-zero exit it reports today is the stub rule and nothing else; and `boot-guard.sh` exits 1 with a named case when one expectation is flipped. Orphan check after both: `pgrep -fl "next start"` empty.

- **CI.** The e2e stage is now two steps: **E1's shell smoke (green)** and **the legacy Sydney journeys (red until F-d / Phase 1)**, which run rather than being disabled, per the HANDOFF §11 S0 rule. `playwright.config.ts` gained one `testIgnore` so the legacy config does not collect the smoke, which has servers of its own.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (1c — signup, UK mobile, passwordless catch; L-007 Phase 1)

**`src/modules/onboarding-parent/`** — the inside on F-b's connector: `types.ts` (rewritten: `SignupContext` · `ParentSignupInput` · `ParentProfileStore` + its error details · the three action shapes · the four prop types), `index.ts`; `lib/normalise-uk-mobile.ts` (ADR-102, prefix from `LOCALE`), `parent-signup-schema.ts` (zod, one message per field, floor from `SECURITY.password`), `signup-copy.ts` (04 §8's promise line verbatim), `post-signup-destination.ts` (`03.36`), `safe-next-path.ts`, `signup-context-from-query.ts`, `auth-callback-destination.ts`, `record-signup-consent.ts` (AGR-01 → `client-tos` + `privacy-policy` through `platform/consent`), `open-position-from-lead.ts` (P-2 + autofire), `send-parent-welcome.ts`, `parent-profile-store-registry.ts` + `configure-` + `default-` + `memory-parent-profile-store.ts`; `actions/sign-up-parent-action.ts` · `sign-in-action.ts` · `request-password-reset-action.ts`; `components/ParentSignupForm.tsx` · `SignInForm.tsx` · `ForgotPasswordForm.tsx` · `AuthShell.tsx`; seven `__tests__` files (142 tests, 6 `it.fails` pins); `README.md`. **Route files (thin, ≤ 40 lines, one connector):** `src/app/(auth)/{layout,signup/page,signup/parent/page,login/page,forgot-password/page,reset-password/page}.tsx`, `src/app/(funnel)/matchmaking/signup/page.tsx` (legacy `MatchmakingSignupClient.tsx` deleted), `src/app/api/auth/callback/route.ts` (rebuilt over `auth.handleAuthCallback`). **One list extended:** `public-site/__tests__/public-site.copy.test.ts` `OWNED_ROUTE_FILES` (+7 routes). **Not touched:** `auth/**` (its `/set-password` + `SetPasswordForm` reused at `/reset-password`), `src/boot/**`, `src/instrumentation.ts`, `platform/**`, `shared-types/**`, `config/**`, `supabase/**`, `(public)/**`, `(funnel)/results/**`, `matching/**`, `areas/**`, `(auth)/signup/nanny` (S-X-07, Phase 2).

**Design decisions recorded in the L-007 `1c` entry:** INTERNAL refusals carry no reason to the form (01 §4a strips them) — the reason is logged, the form shows the generic line beside its standing sign-in link; the profile write is a module registry that fails closed (the same shape as every other port) rather than a table insert under service scope; the lead conversion degrades to S-P-03 state 0 rather than failing the signup; consent is two `consent_records` rows (one per document) under one AGR-01 checkpoint; a UK mobile is the config prefix + `7` + nine digits (stricter than C-7, which also admits landlines); the `(auth)` chrome lives in `onboarding-parent` (nobody owned it; recorded).

**Defect closed, with the number re-measured.** `piiSafeString` refused **2 904 / 20 000** random v4 uuids (**14.52 %**) on this tree before the change — 1d measured 2 944 / 20 000 independently. Cause: `piiSafeString` reaches PII through `scrubFreeText`, whose phone pass runs over the whole string rather than token by token, so it never saw `looksLikePii`'s long-standing uuid exemption; a uuid whose hex groups hold a 9–15-digit run across the hyphens reads as a phone number. 03 §9.3 calls `P.id` "a uuid or an opaque id", and under a unit of work the refusal failed the whole C row — `chooseSlot` would have failed about one call in seven against real booking ids.

It arrived with `1b` (PR #22), whose entry records `build` **not run**. It also takes `check:boot-guard` to 4 / 6 (the two "boots and serves" cases have no `.next` to start) and means the **Vercel preview will fail** until someone owns it. See the L-007 follow-ups.
