## Files created / modified in the current unit (BUILD-FIX — a server-only module no longer reaches a client bundle; `1b` regression)

**Branch `build-fix-170926-1`**, own worktree, off **`bd0f5da` = `origin/main`**; **`c43a206` (P1-WIRE-2, PR #23) merged down** after it landed, so `merge-base --is-ancestor` reports CURRENT. ADR-123 build task: no review battery, no skills. Registered in `../OPERATIONS/BRANCHES.md`.

**The defect, reproduced on `origin/main` `bd0f5da` in a fresh checkout before anything was changed** (`npm run build`, verbatim):

```
./src/modules/auth/lib/elevated-client.ts
Error:
  x You're importing a component that needs server-only. That only works in a Server Component which is not
  | supported in the pages/ directory.
 ,-[src/modules/auth/lib/elevated-client.ts:1:1]
 4 | import "server-only";
   : ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
Import trace for requested module:
./src/modules/auth/lib/elevated-client.ts → ./src/modules/auth/lib/supabase-auth-driver.ts →
./src/modules/auth/index.ts → ./src/modules/matching/lib/connect-decision.ts →
./src/modules/matching/matching.stub.ts → ./src/modules/matching/index.ts →
./src/modules/public-site/components/HomeHero.tsx → ./src/modules/public-site/index.ts →
./src/components/layout/MiniFooter.tsx
```

**Cause, one line:** a **client component imports a connector barrel**. `MiniFooter` (`"use client"`) takes `isPublicSitePath` from the `public-site` barrel; `1b` made that barrel import `matching`'s connector (ADR-126, which 01 §2.3 allows), `matching`'s reaches `auth`'s, and `auth`'s module-level binding **is** the real Supabase inside — so the `server-only` service-role client lands in the client compilation. The lazy `await import("./elevated-client")` in the driver does not save it: webpack still compiles a dynamic-import chunk into the client graph.

**Second break of the same class, behind the first** (webpack stops at the `server-only` error, so `main` never reports it): `AreaCombobox` · `QuickMatchResults` · `NannyPreviewCard` (all `1b`, all `"use client"`) take `formatAreaLabel` from the `areas` barrel → `areas.stub.ts` → `@/modules/platform` → `unit-of-work` → **`node:async_hooks`** ("Reading from `node:async_hooks` is not handled by plugins"). `platform`'s own barrel header says **"Client-safe"**; P1-WIRE's static `node:` import made that false.

- **`src/components/layout/MiniFooter.tsx`** — one import: `isPublicSitePath` now comes from its leaf (`@/modules/public-site/lib/is-public-site-path`), not the connector barrel. The file is legacy tree (`eslint.legacy-paths.json`), so no boundary rule applies to it; when F-d crosses it into a module the lint will make the question explicit again.
- **`src/modules/public-site/index.ts`** — **comment only.** `1a`'s "client-safe by construction" note was true on the narrower test of `@/modules/config/server` and `1b` made it false; it is corrected rather than left to mislead the next reader, because the barrel re-exports screens that import `matching` and **cannot** be client-safe again.
- **`src/modules/platform/unit-of-work/lib/create-unit-of-work.ts`** — `node:async_hooks` becomes a **type-only** import plus one lazy `await import(/* webpackIgnore: true */ "node:async_hooks")` at first use, so the specifier enters no compilation and the barrel's "client-safe" claim is true again. `AsyncLocalStorage` is created on the first `withUnitOfWork` call instead of at `createUnitOfWork`; `current()` answers `undefined` before that, which is what it answered anyway (no storage = no unit of work open). No signature changed, so `src/boot/**` and `auth/**` are untouched.
- **`src/__tests__/client-server-boundary.test.ts`** — new, the pin (below).
- **Not touched:** `auth/**` (the defect's file is correct as written — `server-only` is exactly the guard 07 §7 item 3 asks for) · `shared-types/**` · `supabase/**` · `src/boot/**` · `/api/health` (S5b) · `config/**` · every module inside · eslint config · `package.json` · `next.config.mjs`.

**The test that proves it.** `src/__tests__/client-server-boundary.test.ts` walks the graph **webpack** walks, from every `"use client"` file, and asserts none reaches `server-only` or a `node:` builtin. Three details make it measure the real bundle rather than a plausible one: type-only imports are erased (walking them reports eight violations that do not exist — `matching/types.ts` imports `type { Session }` from `auth`); `import()` **is** an edge (the defect hid behind one); `"use server"` is a stop (Next replaces those with a client reference). **Red on `main` — 6 offenders, the `server-only` chain plus five `node:async_hooks` ones — green here.** `npm test` now fails in ~1 s where `next build` failed in four minutes, and only after a Vercel preview had already gone red.

**Gates, exit codes measured in the foreground on the merged tree (`c43a206` down):** **`build` 0** (the point of the unit) · **`check:boot-guard` 0 — back to 6 / 6** · `check:bundle-secrets` **0** (177 client chunks, no server-only name, no `stub-stripe`) · `typecheck` **0** · `lint` **0** (133 legacy warnings, **0** on any file this unit touched) · `prettier --check .` **0** · `vitest run` **0 — 3 007 passed + 7 expected-fail / 3 014 across 214 files** (+4 tests, +1 file) · `lint:boundaries` **0** · `check:allowed-imports` **0** · `check:claude-md` **0** · `check:config-literals` **0** · `check:env-reads` **0** · `check:prod-guard` **0** · `env:check` **0** · `crons:check` **0**. Accepted red: `check:banned-words` **1** (ADR-124 — legacy hits, **0** on this unit's files). `build` is measured with the CI workflow's placeholder `env:` block, as every unit since `1a` has measured it.

**`build` is green on this branch and `main` is still red until this PR merges** — the trunk row in `../OPERATIONS/BRANCHES.md` carries the ★ until then.

Supporting, one export each: `available-slots.ts` · `call-list-item.ts` · `booking-from-row.ts` · `rule-from-row.ts` · `actor-columns.ts` · `subject-columns.ts` · `book-slot-args.ts` · `displace-to.ts` · `patch-booking.ts` · `resolve-calendar.ts` · `slot-start.ts` · `scheduling-failure.ts`. `types.ts` gains the row aliases, `HeldFor`, `SchedulingReads`, `SchedulingContext`, `ResolvedCalendar`, `AvailableSlotsInput`, `BookSlotResult` and two reasons 03 §3.4 names as **codes** without a reason (`NOT_FOUND` · `FORBIDDEN`). `scheduling.stub.ts` takes the widened `hold` and enforces I-10 on a held row.

**Contract corrections this unit made, each grounded rather than preferred.** (1) **`Scheduling.hold` carries `{ kind, subject }`.** 03 §3.2 writes `hold(slotId, actor)`, but §3.2's own `ACTIVE_STATUSES` includes `'held'` and §3.3 **I-10** says one active booking per subject — so a held row _has_ a subject by the contract's own words, and 02 §4.4 row 4 makes `subject_type`, `subject_id` and `kind` all NOT NULL while `book_slot()` refuses a hold whose subject or kind does not match (`HOLD_NOT_YOURS`). A two-argument `hold` can write no row at all; the invariant won over the signature. (2) **`SchedulingReason` gains `NOT_FOUND` and `FORBIDDEN`** — 03 §3.4 names both as codes with no reason beside them while 03 §1 rule 4 says a contract's failures close over a reason union; the reasons mirror the codes rather than borrowing `CALENDAR_UNKNOWN`, which would tell a caller the calendar is gone when one booking is simply absent. (3) **`removeAvailabilityRule` closes a rule with `effectiveTo`** rather than deleting it — 03 §1.4's `Query` has no `delete`, and `effectiveTo` is the field 03 §3.2 puts on `AvailabilityRule` for exactly "no longer in force"; the audit trail survives, which a delete would have thrown away.

**Displacement landed, and the reason it could is worth recording.** S5 noted that a hold _is_ a `bookings` row and concluded sequence 1 "has no path"; `1d` pinned displacement `it.fails`. Both were about the **hold**, not the move. `book_slot()` (`0009`) already takes `p_displace_to` and does the whole of I-2 / I-3 / I-13 under the calendar lock — it simply does not _search_ for the free slot, deliberately, so that I-6 and I-7 keep one home in `lib/generate-slots.ts` and `lib/next-free-slot.ts`. So `lib/displace-to.ts` computes the target with the same pure function the stub uses and hands it in: **one RPC, one transaction, ADR-127**, no `displace_booking()` definer needed and **no migration written**. A parent taking a displaceable slot never holds it first (the partial unique index would refuse the hold), which is why the hold's absence never blocked the path.

The methods: `link-methods.ts` · `access-methods.ts` · `trial-methods.ts` · `dfy-methods.ts` · `webhook-method.ts`, composed by `create-payments.ts`; `create-payments-jobs.ts` is the only file in the group that does I/O for a cron. The seam: `spine-store.ts` (the port), `db-spine-store.ts` (over `auth`'s data port, **every by-id read keyed** — ADR-131 (1)), `memory-spine-store.ts` (the double, mirroring the three RPCs of `0010` rule for rule). Supporting: `deps.ts` · `fail.ts` · `carry-store-error.ts` · `family-uuid.ts` · `mint-link-ref.ts` · `parse-link-ref.ts` · `blank-spine-row.ts` · `add-days.ts` · `add-months.ts` · `format-money.ts` · `is-duplicate-key-error.ts` · `emit-money-events.ts` · `send-app-ready.ts` · `send-payment-link-email.ts` · `send-trial-reminder.ts` · `load-money-page.ts`; the second binding `payments-jobs-registry.ts` · `configure-payments-jobs.ts` · `default-payments-jobs.ts`.
- **`src/modules/payments/{components,actions}/`** — `MoneyStandingPanel.tsx` (the shared body, one live region for a11y-19) · `AskMatchmakerNote.tsx` · `BundlePage.tsx` (S-P-10) · `SelfServePage.tsx` (S-P-11) · `BundleStatusPage.tsx` (S-P-12); `start-checkout-action.ts` · `open-portal-action.ts`, both resolving the family from the **session**.
- **`src/app/parent/{bundle,subscribe,subscription}/page.tsx`** — S-P-10 / S-P-11 / S-P-12, thin (05 §7 rule 5). The Sydney `SubscribeClient.tsx`, `SubscriptionClient.tsx` and the whole `subscription/cancel/` flow are **deleted**: they read Sydney columns through `@/lib/supabase`, revealed the amounts before the call, and used the word ADR-082 rules out.
- **`src/boot/wire-payments.ts`** + **`wire-purchase-paths.ts`** + two members on `BootPort` + two lines in `wire-ports.ts` — **P1-WIRE's owed wiring, landed.** Its blocker was removed at the source rather than worked around: `stub-stripe`'s `constant-time-equals.ts` reached `node:crypto`, and `src/instrumentation.ts` is built for the edge runtime too (`src/middleware.ts` makes one exist). The compare is now pure arithmetic over a fixed 256-character window, the stub touches no Node builtin, and the static import is safe in both runtimes. `check:bundle-secrets` (177 chunks, no `stub-stripe`), `check:prod-guard` and `check:boot-guard` all measured green after.
- **`src/app/api/_lib/run-cron.ts`** — one **additive** optional `handler` parameter, so a shell that has an inside hands it over and the 22 that do not keep answering `no-handler-registered`. The `CRON_SECRET` check still lives in exactly one place. **`src/app/api/cron/{payment-due-sweep,expire-trials,trial-reminders,expire-past-due,expire-cancelled-subscriptions}/route.ts`** each gained their handler line.
- **`src/modules/config/prices.ts`** (+ `config.values.test.ts`) — one key, `trialReminderDaysBefore: 5`. 03 §5.4.4 fixes the T-5 reminder and no key held the day count; a literal in a job is what L4 forbids.
- **`src/modules/payments/types.ts`** — `MoneyPageLoad`, and **one member on the error vocabulary**: `E_STORE`. 03 §5.3 names no reason for "the database refused"; the alternatives were a reason that means something else (`E_PROVIDER` says the provider failed) or widening the details type to `string`, and on a money path the reason is what the runbook triages on.
- **Tests (95 new claims across six files).** `payments.money.test.ts` (the bill, the ADR-100 caps, the presets from config) · `payments.dispatch.test.ts` (every row of §5.4.3, and every row it does not have) · `payments.inside.test.ts` (the nine methods: who may call each, what each writes, what each refuses) · `payments.webhook.test.ts` (verification first, the ledger before dispatch, a replay changes nothing, an unresolved event is a 200) · `payments.jobs.test.ts` (the five sweeps, each re-run idempotent) · `payments.copy.test.ts` (**every string a parent reads**, generated over all nine standings) · `payments.screens.test.ts` · `purchase-paths/__tests__/stub-stripe.guards.test.ts` (the three 07 §5.5 layers and the rewritten compare) · plus eight rows in `src/boot/__tests__/{boot,ports.fail-closed}.test.ts`.
- **Not touched:** `supabase/**` (**no migration in this unit** — `0010_money.sql` already carries the spine, the ledger and the three RPCs) · `shared-types/**` · `auth/**` · `platform/**` · `connections` / `placements` / `call-layer` (`1g`) · `positions` / `matching` (`1e`) · `admin/**` · `app/**` · `src/instrumentation.ts` · eslint config · `package.json`.

**What runs for real, and what runs against the stub.** For real: the spine and the ledger (`parent_subscriptions`, `payment_events` through `auth`'s data port, keyed reads), the three money RPCs of `0010`, the access standing and the gate, the idempotency, the events, the `app-ready` / `bundle-payment-link` / `trial-reminder` sends, the five crons, and all three screens. Against `stub-stripe`: only the **provider** — customer, link URL, checkout URL, portal URL and the event signature. No Stripe SDK call, no real key, no payout logic (N-2, ADR-022). An admin click on the stub synthesises a `PurchaseEvent` and posts it through the **same** `handleWebhook` a real provider would, so the record, the events and the gate run for real either way.

**Contradictions stopped on rather than guessed.** (1) **The brief asked for a rate limit on the webhook; 07 §8 row 13 forbids one** — "no rate limit (provider retries by design); signature + idempotency are the control". A limiter there drops events a provider then stops retrying, which on a money spine loses a payment. The document won and the absence is pinned as a test. ADR-134 governs routes that _have_ a limit row; this one has none, and neither do the crons (the Bearer secret is their control). (2) **03 §5.2 says of `portal` that "cancellation stays in-app", but `PurchasePath` has no cancel method** — a behaviour the document names and the contract gives no road to. Rather than invent a connector method on a money path, stopping the monthly payments is the hosted portal's and the claim is pinned `it.fails`. (3) **AC-A-41 asks `payment-due-sweep` to raise an open `admin_notifications.payment_due`; no module owns that table** (01 §2.3 names no writer and `payments` may not reach past `comms`). The `payment.due` event is emitted; the notification is pinned `it.fails`. (4) **03 §5.5 lists `ref?` among the money events' props; `platform/events/schemas/purchase.ts` has no such prop**, so a `LinkRef` cannot travel on an event without the emit being refused. Recorded for `platform`'s owner.

- **`src/boot/`** — new `wire-positions.ts`; `wire-ports.ts` (**one line + the order note**, additive) · `types.ts` (`BootPort` + `"positions"`) · `__tests__/{boot,ports.fail-closed}.test.ts`.
- **`/api/health`** — **not touched.** ADR-135 was already satisfied by S5b's route: the response is built in the route (not through `toResponse`), 503 when `db` is `"failed"`, body unchanged, E1's smoke assertion flipped and the 200 + `ok` unit test kept. Verified, not rebuilt.
- **Not touched:** `src/middleware.ts` (the whole gate is the pure `gateDecision` it already calls — which is why the rule landed in one place) · `supabase/**` · `shared-types/**` · `config/**` · `platform/**` · `positions/**` · `matching/**` · `call-layer/**` · `scheduling/**` · `admin/**` (`1f`) · `payments` / `purchase-paths` / `access-gate` (`1h`) · every `(public)` / `(funnel)` / `(auth)` route body · eslint config · `package.json`.

**Design decisions recorded in the L-007 AUTH-2 entry.** (1) **One method, not two** — the anonymous catch and the forgot half are indistinguishable from outside under ADR-132, so two methods would be one function with a dead argument. (2) **The recovery email is Supabase's own template, not `comms`** — three measured blockers, below. (3) **A provider failure answers `ok`** — surfacing it would restore the enumeration oracle; logged with `ALERT_PROVIDER_DOWN`, scoped to one file. (4) **A limiter outage refuses** — the opposite of the public reads, because for an auth surface fail-open hands back the very thing the limit exists to stop. (5) **`memoryPositionStore` is refused in production**, the same rule `wire-scheduling.ts` and `wire-call-layer.ts` state.

**★ Foundations corrections found by building.** (1) **The reset email cannot go through `comms`, and ADR-136's instruction cannot be executed on this trunk** — three independent blockers, each measurable: `auth` may import `platform` only (01 §2.3, enforced by `lint:boundaries`), so it has no arrow to `comms`; 03 §8.2's 46-id registry names **no** password-reset or set-password template, and `08.05` already places the auth emails in Supabase's own dashboard templates; and ADR-136's `Recipient = { userId }` is **not implemented** — today's `Recipient` still requires an `email`, so "pass the user id, not an address" has nothing to pass it to. Supabase's `resetPasswordForEmail` also gives the no-enumeration property by construction, which a hand-rolled send over `generateLink` would have had to re-implement under a new service-role use nobody ruled. **Owner: the 03 §8 owner + whoever rules ADR-136's implementation.** (2) **04 §6.1 S-X-08's "known email with no password → S-X-09" cannot be executed as written** — routing that address differently _is_ the enumeration oracle 07 §4 and ADR-132 forbid. What survives is the promise behind it (never an error, always offered a link), delivered by one refusal plus the reassuring line. **04 §6.1's S-X-08 cell wants the same amendment ADR-132 made to §6.1's duplicate-email cell.** (3) **`1c`'s pin (3) was un-closeable as written** and was rewritten against the ruling, recorded in the test file.

**Security review (`security-reviewer` only, the brief's exception): 0 CRITICAL · 1 HIGH · 2 MEDIUM · 1 LOW. The HIGH and the LOW are closed; both MEDIUMs are recorded with why they were not built.** **HIGH (closed) — the reset request had no rate limit.** Until this unit the action was inert, so the absence cost nothing; wired to a real send it is a way to fill one person's inbox with recovery links and to spend the project's whole outbound email quota. Now limited per hashed address under `SECURITY.rateLimits.authPerEmail`, before any send, with the response unchanged either way. **LOW (closed)** — `sign-in-action.ts`'s header still described the gap this unit closed. **MEDIUM-1 (recorded)** — the known-vs-unknown _timing_ property lives inside GoTrue and cannot be tested from this repo; it wants one comparison against the preview project, which this unit does not deploy to. **MEDIUM-2 (recorded)** — an `amr` shape the module does not recognise fails **closed** (a genuine recovery session would be bounced, never wrongly admitted); an alert was not added because there is no signal that distinguishes "a shape we do not know" from "not a recovery session", and a warning on every ordinary sign-in would be noise, not a control.

**Gates, exit codes measured in the foreground on the merged tree:** `typecheck` **0** · `lint` **0** · `prettier --check .` **0** · `vitest run` **0 — 3 224 passed + 7 expected-fail / 3 231 across 229 files** · `lint:boundaries` **0** · `check:allowed-imports` **0** · `check:config-literals` **0** · `check:env-reads` **0** · `check:claude-md` **0** · `check:prod-guard` **0** · `check:boot-guard` **0 — 6 / 6** · `check:audit` **0** · **`npm run build` 0**. `check:banned-words` **1** — the accepted red (ADR-124); **0 hits on any file this unit created**, the two under `auth/**` being the pre-existing `README.md` "booked" (P1-WIRE's unit-of-work paragraph) and `SetPasswordForm.tsx` "upgrade" (S4's). The build needs the placeholder environment to collect page data (`set -a; . ./.env.test; set +a` plus a `RESEND_API_KEY` placeholder), both local-only, as `1e` measured.

**Not touched:** `src/modules/positions/lib/{create-positions-slice,advance,position-transitions}.ts` and the position store · `src/modules/comms/**` · `wire-positions.ts` / `wire-comms.ts` (all P1-STORES) · `payments` / `purchase-paths` insides · `access-gate`'s inside (`1h`) · any migration · `config/**` except reading it · `platform/**` · eslint config · `package.json`.

**Design decisions.** (1) **The claim screen and the public preview are one view** — the claim screen _is_ the preview with a signed-in visitor, and two files means two copies of the five states and the no-enumeration rule. (2) **Claimed and revoked share one screen**: `get_invite_preview` answers only for `pending` rows, and if the page could tell a stranger "claimed" from "never existed" a script could walk the token space for live families. (3) **`createInvite` is idempotent, not regenerating** — "resend" means the same link again; a second token would silently invalidate the one the recipient holds, which is rotation under another name. (4) **Rows 7 and 8 take their facts on the input**: `positions` may import neither `payments` nor `app`, `app` may import neither `payments` nor `access-gate`, and `access-gate` may be imported by nothing at all — so the reads happen in the route file and `getJourneySteps` / `loadParentJourney` each gained one additive optional parameter. The first draft of `app-rail-facts.ts` sat inside the module and `lint:boundaries` refused it, correctly. (5) **The `app` stub does not fake the writes** — a seed-backed mint would hand out a token that exists on no row.

**Gaps this unit found and pinned rather than papered over.** ★ `0012` ships **no definer for the invite mint or the revoke** while 07 §5.2 says links and invites are written only by RPCs, so both run service-scoped with `invite-authorisation.ts` as the only authorisation — a **`0019`** wants `create_child_invite` / `revoke_child_invite`. ★ `children` has **no creator column**, so a nanny cannot read back the child she created and 04 §4.4 c1's path is unreachable for her. ★ `connect_child_invite` does not call `set_access_window` itself, so the ADR-084 call happens just outside its transaction. ★ **ADR-134's named fail-open allow-list does not exist in `config/security.ts`** — nothing is on it, so the invite lookup fails closed, which is also right on the merits here.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (S5c — migration `0019`, the write definers; L-007)

**Migration + its twin + its suites**

- `supabase/migrations/0019_write-definers.sql` — six definers, one column, one trigger, the fourth arm, and a metadata verify block that says it is metadata.
- `supabase/rollbacks/0019_write-definers.rollback.sql` — the twin. It **restores `0012`'s three-arm `user_has_child_access()`** rather than dropping it: every app-table policy reads that predicate, so a `drop` is an outage, not a rollback. The one thing it loses that cannot be recovered is `children.created_by_user_id`, and the header says so.
- `supabase/__tests__/rpc-0019.test.ts` — **new `int.rpc-0019`, 44 tests, every one of which invokes a function.** S5b's lesson on `0017` was that 151 metadata assertions passed over a function that could not insert a row; `0019`'s own verify block therefore stops at metadata deliberately and this suite is where the claims live.
- `supabase/__tests__/constraints.test.ts` — `db.constraints` grows by 11 claims over what `0019` added.
- `src/modules/shared-types/database.types.ts` — regenerated with `npm run types:generate` against a local stack.

**The three stores' write paths**

- `src/boot/db-position-store.ts` · `db-connection-store.ts` · `db-placement-store.ts` — each `put` is now one `rpc()`. `writeSchedule` is **gone** from the position store: the roster travels in the same call, which it must, because the port allows exactly one RPC per unit of work.
- `src/boot/__tests__/db-write-definers.test.ts` (new) · `db-position-store.test.ts` · `fixtures/fake-schema-port.ts` (learns `upsert_position` so the round-trip claims stay claims about state) · `boot.test.ts` (P1-STORES' pin flipped, and the thing it was pinning kept as a live assertion).
- `scripts/ci/boot-guard.sh` — case 7: the production environment's writes reach the driver rather than the port's refusal.
- `src/modules/app/__tests__/child-linking.pins.test.ts` — `1i`'s 07 §5.2 pin split into the half that is now true and the half that is not.

**Design decisions worth carrying**

1. **`EXECUTE` is `service_role` for the three stage-writers and `authenticated` for the two invite functions, and the split is not arbitrary.** The stage-writers take `stage` as an argument and `0006` §4 says a parent may never touch `stage`, `call_*` or `precheck_*`; their callers are cascades and jobs with no session at all, so `auth.uid()` is null for every one of them and `is_privileged_writer()` is the only honest authority test. The invite functions are the opposite: a parent mints for her own child, and a null `auth.uid()` is refused outright.
2. **`p_columns jsonb`, merged with `jsonb_populate_record` over the stored row, then written through a static named column list.** An omitted key keeps its value — which is the semantics the stores already had — every value is cast through the table's own column types, and there is no dynamic SQL anywhere. The reserved keys are stripped, including every `call_*` key, so `upsert_call_mirror()` stays the one writer of the call's state.
3. **The stage-model uniqueness is raised by name inside each function**, before the partial unique index can answer with a bare 23505. The indexes stay the backstop and `db.constraints` asserts they are still there, because a named refusal alone is a read-then-check that loses a race.
4. **★ A trigger that reads `is_privileged_writer()` must be `SECURITY INVOKER`.** Written first as a definer, `children_stamp_creator` stamped nothing — `0000`'s own comment says `is_privileged_writer()` is true for "any SECURITY DEFINER owned by" the schema owner. Found by invoking it, not by reading it, and asserted twice now.
5. **★ `insert … returning` on `children` is refused for every client role, and always was.** `children_access_select` reads `user_has_child_access(id)`, a STABLE definer that queries `public.children`; a STABLE function sees the statement's start snapshot, so the row being inserted is invisible to it. `0019` does not change that and could not. The app never hit it because `insertChild` runs at service scope. Whoever wires the module half needs to know.

**What the next unit must know**

1. **The three stores are wired; the invite and payment functions are NOT.** `0019` ships six functions and rewires three. `db-child-linking-store.ts` still writes `child_invites` as a table write at service scope, and `webhook-method.ts` still makes its two table writes — both pinned, both named. Wiring the invite pair is **not** a rename: they derive authority from `auth.uid()`, so the calls must move to session scope.
2. **`1i`'s 04 §4.4 c1 pin stays RED on purpose.** The schema half landed; the module half (`ChildFacts` gaining `createdByUserId`, `mayMint` accepting it, `insertChild` writing it, the store selecting it) did not, and flipping `mayMint` before those four would make a green test a claim about nothing.
3. **`1i`'s third pin is untouched**: `connect_child_invite` still does not call `set_access_window` inside its own transaction. `0019` was not asked for it and did not take it.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (2b — the verification wizard behind `vetting-providers` / `stub-manual`; L-008 Phase 2)

**First act — the ruling, before any code.** Kickoff §4.1 ruled as **ADR-153**: right-to-work is a parallel section with no level effect; the gate is `VETTING.requiredChecksByLevel`, so reversing it is one config line; no cross-check join; B-20 · 03 §12 item 14 · 04 §10 item 22 closed. Amend-first for the two facts the foundations lacked: **ADR-154** (`0022`; `vetting_submissions.evidence_id`; I-V1's timing; contact "saved" = `verified`; the store's `upsert` takes the evidence) and **ADR-155** (the data port's `putObject` / `removeObject`). Doc lines: 04 §4.1 row 12 · §6.3 S-N-10 · §10 item 22; 03 §1.4 · §4.2 · §4.3 · §12 item 14; 02 §4.3 · I-V1 · §6 row `0022` · §7 four rows; 01 §6.3; 07 §5.1 rule 5; DECISIONS B-20.

**Migration + its twin + its suites**

- `supabase/migrations/0022_verification-wizard.sql` — `evidence_id` (NOT NULL, unique; a table with rows and no key is **refused**, not backfilled) · `verification_submission_columns()` (the static per-section list) · `submit_verification_evidence()` (`ON CONFLICT (evidence_id) DO NOTHING` + re-select — the retry race; per-section evidence-type check; identity needs the caller's OWN `biometric-notice` consent row; a selfie is a second submission, not a second attempt) · `save_verification_contact()` · `claim_verification_processing()` (I-V4) · `apply_vetting_check_result()` (`service_role` only; `STALE_SUBMISSION` recency guard per evidence type; never `level` / `dbs_outcome` / the cross-check / the sweep-owned Update Service columns — the verify block asserts it, plus BYPASSRLS ownership and the exact `search_path=""`). **`database-reviewer`: 4 HIGH · 3 MEDIUM · 2 LOW, all closed in-unit** (H1 exact search_path · H2 the on-conflict race · H3 the recency guard · H4 ownership · M1 fail-loud backfill · M2 the Update Service wording + regex · M3 evidence type ↔ section · L1 regex anchor · L2 UTC date).
- `supabase/rollbacks/0022_verification-wizard.rollback.sql` — the twin; one transaction; loses the idempotency key on a database that has rows (none anywhere).
- `supabase/__tests__/rpc-0022.test.ts` — **`int.rpc-0022`, 18 tests, every one invokes a function** (RED first: 15 failed on the missing function, then 3 more for the reviewer's closures). `supabase/__tests__/storage-rls.test.ts` — **`int.storage-rls`, 4**: a nanny writes under her own prefix + section, a foreign prefix or unknown section is refused, **another nanny cannot read the object and neither can its owner** (07 §5.3 rule 2), no user may update or delete, an admin reads. `constraints.test.ts` +5. Cycle on the local stack: `supabase db reset` (forwards from empty, `0000`–`0022` incl. `0020` after the main merge-down) → the rollback twin → re-apply → **18 + 4 + 64**. `database.types.ts` regenerated with `npm run types:generate` (+35 lines vs `main`, all `0022`'s).
- **★ Measured, not read:** PostgreSQL refuses `INSERT … RETURNING` on `storage.objects` where no SELECT policy admits the row (the bucket has none for a user by design), but the storage API's own upload does not return the row — probed end to end against the local stack: an authenticated own-prefix upload succeeds, a foreign prefix is refused, the owner's download is refused, the service role removes. And the stack's `protect_objects_delete` trigger refuses a direct DELETE for every role, so deletion goes through the Storage API (`removeObject`).

- `__tests__/` — **RED first, all failed on missing modules:** `pure` 12 · `schemas` 12 · `inside` 12 · `upload` 5 · `copy` (every component + lib file against the glossary §8 nanny list; the wizard says a person reviews) · the `repo` and `fail-closed` suites refreshed · `fixtures/tiny-jpeg.ts` (a real one-pixel JPEG from `sharp` — the magic-bytes fixture was rightly refused by the re-encode). → **224 green.**

**Boot + routes + config + shared files**

- `src/boot/db-vetting-store.ts` (session-scope submit over `0022`; service-scope ledger reads, named; `recordDecision` refuses `decision-not-built` — `2c`) · `db-verification-store.ts` (`nannies` → `verification_status` view under RLS; the three definers; `apply_vetting_check_result` at service scope) · `wire-verification.ts` (+ the contact writer injected from `onboarding-nanny`'s store — the `1i` shape) · `wire-ports.ts` · `unwired-ports.ts` (two rows fewer) · `__tests__/boot.test.ts` · `__tests__/db-verification-stores.test.ts` (**8** — written after the adapters, against `db-nanny-stores.test.ts`'s pattern; the one place this unit wrote code before its test) · the two fake ports.
- Routes: `nanny/onboarding-verification/page.tsx` (three reads, one component, `?step=` may name an open step, `DistrictCombobox` as the S-N-04 slot) · `nanny/verification/page.tsx` · `nanny/verify/page.tsx` · `admin/viewer/[id]/verification/page.tsx` (re-pointed at S-N-09 read-only through `verification.getStatus` behind `requireRole("admin")`). **Deleted:** the 2 587-line Sydney `OnboardingVerificationClient.tsx`, `VerifyLaterEscape.tsx`, `VerificationPageClient.tsx` + `sections/` (WWCC).
- `config/vetting.ts` (ADR-153: `L4` drops `right-to-work-passport` + its `[unverified]`; `rightToWorkEvidence`; `dbsCertificateNumber` 12 digits · `shareCode` 9 chars — both `[unverified]`; `processing`; `biometricNotice` disclosures) · `onboarding-nanny/index.ts` (one export, `DistrictCombobox`) · `scripts/ci/limiter-call-sites.allow.json` (`verificationSubmissions` removed — consumed) · `scripts/ci/boot-guard.sh` (one named allowance: `stub-manual` is 03 §4.4's day-one human road, not a test double an env name selects) · `public/katie-manifest.json` (the prebuild's own regeneration: migration 23).

**Design decisions worth carrying**

1. **The gate is a config row.** ADR-153 stands on 03 §4's shape: `requiredChecksByLevel` decides which evidence types gate a level; `deriveLevel` (`2c`) reads it; the wizard never does.
2. **One definer writes both tables** (ADR-154 (2)): `submit_verification_evidence()` inserts the ledger row and the section's submission columns in one transaction, so the store's `upsert` takes the evidence and ADR-127's one-RPC rule holds without a unit of work.
3. **The scan runs before the write.** 07 §5.3 rule 3 says a failed scan deletes the object; scanning first means an infected file is never at rest, and `removeObject` is reserved for a submission that fails after its uploads.
4. **`stub-manual`'s only outcome is needs-admin**, so binding it in every environment answers REVIEW-1 M-9 by construction, and the boot-guard's stub check gains one named allowance rather than a reworded line.
5. **★ Two measured facts** about the stack (above): `INSERT … RETURNING` vs a bucket with no SELECT policy, and the delete-protection trigger.

- `supabase/rollbacks/0021_nanny-onboarding.rollback.sql` — the twin; loses no data, loses the road.

The role helpers run each statement inside a savepoint so an expected refusal is observable.

- `lib/` (32 files, one export each): the two store registries / defaults / `configure*` / memory doubles; `funnel-options` · `funnel-steps` · `profile-steps`; the schemas (`nanny-application` · `nanny-portfolio` · `availability-field` · `nanny-bio` · `nanny-signup` · `nanny-portal` · `nanny-profile-step-schemas`); `parse-form` · `refuse-funnel`; `carried-token-cookie` · `read-lead-cookie`; `caller-ip-key` · `caller-ip-ua-key` · `email-rate-key` · `consume-funnel-step-limit` · `consume-nanny-signup-limit`; `record-nanny-signup-consent` (AGR-02: `professional-tos` + `privacy-policy`) · `send-nanny-welcome` (`welcome-nanny`, by user id — ADR-136); `is-nanny-profile-complete` · `lead-to-profile` · `nanny-hub-view` · `post-nanny-signup-destination` · `load-nanny-profile` · `load-nanny-hub`.

**Boot + routes + the invite cluster + shared files**

- `src/boot/db-nanny-lead-store.ts` (service scope over `nanny_leads`; the id minted client-side; the "sign in instead" answer from `user_profiles.email` and a converted lead, never the identity provider) · `db-nanny-account-store.ts` (session scope over the three RPCs; the camel → snake column map in one place) · `wire-nanny-onboarding.ts` · `wire-ports.ts` + `types.ts` (`BootPort` gains `nanny-onboarding`) · `__tests__/db-nanny-stores.test.ts` (9).
- Routes: `(public)/childcare-professionals/page.tsx` · `(funnel)/apply/{page,layout}.tsx` · `(auth)/signup/nanny/page.tsx` · `(auth)/signup/page.tsx` (merges the invite cookie into the query the parent connector reads — one line, no connector change) · `nanny/page.tsx` · `nanny/register/page.tsx` · `nanny/apply/page.tsx` (new; the legacy `(public)/nanny/apply/page.tsx` deleted) · `invite/connect/[token]/page.tsx` (`"link"` → `"signup"`). `next.config.mjs`: **the two redirects that contradicted 04 §2.1 are gone** (kickoff §2 debt 1).
- `app/child-linking`: `actions/start-invite-signup-action.ts` (new — S-X-13's Join is a form that mints the invite cookie, ADR-150) · `lib/invite-landing-view.ts` (`link` → `signup`) · `lib/load-invite-landing.ts` (`next=` to the claim route; no token in a query — debts 4 + 10) · `components/InviteLandingPage.tsx` · `actions/claim-invite-action.ts` (`next=`; **one sentence for every non-outage refusal, ADR-151**; the reason logged) · the three suites re-argued to the amended document.
- `config/security.ts` (`SECURITY.carriedTokens`) · `config/matching.ts` (ADR-149: DfE labels, keys unchanged, `[unverified]` struck) · `platform/lib/normalise-uk-mobile.ts` (lifted from `onboarding-parent`; that module's file is now a one-line re-export).

**Design decisions worth carrying**

1. **`is_isolated` is an argument of the account write, never a default reached by omission** (ADR-147). The column defaults `true`; the funnel is the one road that passes `false`. S-X-07 passes `true` whether or not an invite is present, so a bare direct signup is exactly as invisible as an invited one and S-N-22 offers both the same apply road.
2. **One signup action, two roads, differing only in where the person comes from.** `/apply` reads the lead the cookie names (name, email, mobile, district asked once); an invite reads the invite cookie. Everything after — `auth.signUp`, AGR-02, `create_nanny_account`, the welcome, the events — is one path, so the enumeration argument (ADR-132) is one argument.
3. **The carried tokens are unsigned, on ADR-150's argument, and `Secure` follows `x-forwarded-proto`** rather than an environment read: 07 §7 keeps `process.env` to `config`'s two readers, and the literal check enforces it — even in a comment.
4. **`profile_visible` has one writer, in SQL.** `update_nanny_profile()` computes the `03.18` rule and returns it; the TypeScript mirror (`isNannyProfileComplete`) drives only the stepper's state and the memory double, and `int.rpc-0021` holds the two to the same fixtures.
5. **★ zod 4's `z.record` over an enum key is exhaustive.** The availability grid refused every week with a day missing until it became `z.partialRecord` — caught by the RED suite, recorded because it will bite the next grid.

**What the next unit must know**

1. **`2b` starts from the flag, the level and a wired profile read.** `nannyAccountStore.get()` answers the nanny's own two rows; `MATCHING.minVerificationLevel` gates the hub. The wizard writes `verifications`, never `nannies.is_isolated`.
2. **The nanny-mint pin stays RED and named** (`app/__tests__/child-linking.pins.test.ts`): S-N-01 is `2g`'s surface, so `2a` did not own it (kickoff debt 8).
3. **Debt 2 (the nanny's name on parent surfaces) is not landed here** — the profile read this unit built is the nanny's own row under RLS, not the `nanny_public` read a parent surface needs; it stays with `2d`.
4. **Three copies of the hashed rate-limit key helpers now exist** (`api/_lib/ip-key.ts`, `onboarding-parent`, `onboarding-nanny`) — the M-10 shape; one `platform/rate-limit` helper then a mechanical pass, for the checkpoint.
5. **S-N-22's line says "your family"** — `child-linking` has no nanny-keyed read for the linked family's name; 04 §8's `{family}` waits on that read.

**Design decisions worth carrying**

1. **The nanny's form never holds a slot, and that is the contract rather than a shortcut.** 03 §3.2 lists it among the callers that reach `book` with no hold. The parent holds because she is choosing between people's time on a page she may sit on; a hold the nanny cannot see expiring is worse than a `SLOT_TAKEN` she can act on.
2. **The nanny is the session and there is nothing to check.** S-P-02's actions check `positionId` against the caller's own open call because a position id is forgeable; 03 §3.2's `Subject` for a `nanny-commission` booking _is_ the nanny, so taking it from the session is the whole authorisation and no shape exists in which a caller books somebody else's call.
3. **The picker's actions are a tagged union, not an optional `hold`.** "This surface might hold" is not what either surface does; the tag says which one this is and the component cannot call the wrong road.
4. **`createChild`'s unclaimed branch moves neither the trial nor the access window.** ADR-093 ties the trial to a family's first child and ADR-083 / 084 tie the window to a family's youngest — a child with `parent_user_id is null` has no family, so both move at the claim, inside `claimInvite`, where they already did.
5. **`mayMint` and `create_child_invite()` now say the same sentence in two languages**, creator arm included, `parent_user_id is null` clause included. A divergence in either direction is the finding that matters on this surface, so the module test and `int.rpc-0019` assert the same four cases.

**What the next unit must know**

1. **03 §9.3 has no event name for "a nanny added an unclaimed child"**, and the taxonomy is append-only. `2g` emits none rather than borrowing `app.family-in` (whose subject is a parent who does not exist yet); the observable fact is the `invite.sent` one line later. **Question for the planner**, recorded in `app`'s README.
2. **S-N-01's active / passive variant is not built.** 04 §4.1 row 8 drives it from the under-3 signal, which lives on `nanny_leads.lead_signals`; `nannyAccountStore.get()` does not answer it, so the pitch renders the active wording for everyone. Owner: whoever next widens that read (`2d`), or 04 §4.1 row 8 if the variant is dropped.
3. **`admin_notifications` still has no writer.** 04 §4.4 c3's admin notification is the **email** (`admin-commission-booking`, `08.18`) and it is sent; the _row_ AC-A-41 also asks for has no owning module (01 §2.3 names none), which is the same gap `payments` pinned for `payment_due`. One connector closes both.
4. **S-N-02 is gated at the door as well as at the link.** `loadCommissionPage` answers `isolated` and the route sends her to the hub, so hiding the hub link is not the only thing standing between an invited nanny and the pitch (ADR-147). S-N-01 is **not** gated that way: the mint is hers from Settings → Linked children too (04 §4.4 c1 names S-N-21), and isolation is about the matching pool, not about the family she already has.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (2c — the status model, the silent hold, the admin verification queue, the verification comms and the three jobs; L-008 Phase 2)

**Migration `0023_verification-decision-sync.sql`** (+ its rollback twin) — the level's one writer and the four decision definers, with ADR-156 folded in and REVIEW-3's C-1 / R-1 / R-2 / M-5 taken. Additive over `0000`–`0022`; verified **forward from empty on the local stack** (`supabase db reset`, 24 migrations), then **rolled back and re-applied** against the same database with `int.rpc-0023` green on both sides. Not applied to preview or production (B-45 is BAI's; `0021` / `0022` are still not on `bb-ldn-preview`).

- `sync_nanny_verification_state(p_nanny_id, p_required)` (ADR-157 (1)) — the **one** writer of `verifications.level / level_changed_at / suspended_at` and `nannies.verification_level / verification_synced_at / suspended_at`. Derives top-down in SQL against the sections-per-level the caller hands it as `p_required jsonb`, **validated** (every key a level, every value a `verification_section`, L2–L4 non-empty — an emptied list refuses rather than grants), so ADR-153 holds by construction and reversing it is still one config line. Returns `{ from_level, to_level, suspended, released }`; the caller emits exactly one `verification.level-changed`.
- `record_vetting_decision(...)` (ADR-157 (2) / ADR-159) — `apply_vetting_check_result(…, 'admin')`, the note on the ledger, the DBS outcome (`verified` ⇒ `cleared` + cross-check `passed` by the admin, who **is** the check; `rejected adverse` ⇒ `barred` ⇒ L0 + suspended, I-V5; any other rejection ⇒ back to `unset`), then the sync — one transaction.
- `record_update_service_check(...)` (ADR-157 (3)) — the four Update Service columns, the checker refused unless an admin, `new_information` returning the DBS section to `review`; only `no_change` confirms L4 (the B-19 built default).
- `expire_verification_section(...)` (ADR-157 (4)) and `sweep_stale_verification_processing(p_stale_minutes)` (ADR-157 (5) / I-V4).
- **ADR-156 folded:** `payment_events.outcome` (`applied · ignored · unresolved`) written by `apply_payment_event()` in the same transaction; `payment_events_unprocessed_idx` re-keyed on `outcome is null`; a pre-flight refuses to apply over processed rows it cannot attribute.
- **ADR-162 (REVIEW-3 C-1 / R-1):** pool visibility is **one** predicate with one home — `nanny_visible(is_isolated, verification_level)` (IMMUTABLE, so the partial index can carry it) and `nanny_is_visible(nanny_id)`; `is_active_nanny()`, `nanny_public` and `nannies_matching_idx` are all re-created over it. The two pins REVIEW-3 left in `rls-isolation-conjunction.test.ts` **flip green by behaviour**, not by edit.
- **ADR-163 (REVIEW-3 R-2):** `create_nanny_account()` takes `p_user_id` and is EXECUTE to `service_role` only; both signup actions call it at service scope and decide `p_isolated` by road. `int.rpc-0023` proves the authenticated road is refused.
- **REVIEW-3 M-5:** `submit_verification_evidence()` stamps `dbs_update_service_consent_at` with `now()` — the key's _presence_ is the consent, the instant is the server's (0004's rule). The verify block asserts it in the function body.
- **`security-reviewer` H-3:** `vetting_submissions.decided_by` — the admin who recorded a decision, validated against `user_roles` before anything is read and written inside the decision's own transaction. The `vetting.decision-recorded` event carries the same id but 03 §9 makes that emit best-effort, so one dropped emit used to leave the database unable to say who approved a criminal-record check. The 6-argument form of `record_vetting_decision()` is **dropped**, not overloaded.
- **The rollback keeps the level gate.** `security-reviewer` C-1 / H-2: the twin restored `is_active_nanny()` without ADR-162's level term (re-opening the Art 9 read) and `create_nanny_account()`'s `authenticated` grant, and named neither. **Ruling: a rollback undoes a feature, not a measured security fix.** The level term now stays inlined through the rollback — nothing pre-`0023` depended on the gap — while the `create_nanny_account` grant must return, because the code the rollback accompanies calls that signature; it is named as an accepted regression with the hot-patch command written out. `decided_by`'s loss is named too.

**`verification` — the level, the decision, the queue's road, the comms, the jobs**

- `lib/derive-level.ts` + `lib/required-sections-by-level.ts` (new) — the TypeScript half, **pinned equal to the SQL** over the whole matrix in `int.rpc-0023` so the two cannot drift.
- `lib/decide.ts` · `lib/record-update-service-check.ts` · `lib/open-evidence.ts` · `lib/list-queue.ts` · `lib/read-queue-record.ts` · `lib/queue-entry-of.ts` · `lib/admin-overview.ts` (new) — the five admin roads on the connector (ADR-159).
- `lib/require-admin.ts` + `lib/consume-admin-route-limit.ts` (new) — every admin road re-checks `auth.requireRole('admin')` (aal2) **and** consumes `SECURITY.rateLimits.adminRoutes`, its first London consumer; the allow-list entry goes with it. Role first, budget second, work third, on all three roads (`security-reviewer` M-4 closed the one exception).
- `lib/send-verification-outcome.ts` (new) — `08.10`'s outcomes: `verification-approved` on reaching L3 / L4, `verification-action-needed` (+10 min, keyed per nanny + section, cancelled on resubmission) on a rejection, `verification-barred` + the `nanny_barred` admin row on barred. One neutral sentence per outcome; no reason is enumerated beyond the guidance key.
- `lib/sweep-stale-processing.ts` · `lib/sweep-reminders.ts` · `lib/reminder-keys.ts` · `lib/sweep-expiry.ts` (new) — I-V4, `08.11` (offsets 30 min / 24 h / 48 h / 7 d, keyed `verification-reminder:{nanny}:{i}` so every pass is idempotent, cancelled by the sync at L3) and `vetting-expiry`.
- `lib/emit-level-events.ts` · `lib/emit-verification-event.ts` (new); `types.ts`, `index.ts`, `create-verification.ts`, `default-verification.ts`, `unconfigured-verification.ts` follow.
- Suites: `verification.level.test.ts` (8) · `verification.decision.test.ts` (12) · `verification.sweeps.test.ts` (4), **all RED first**.

**`admin-verification` — S-A-16 and S-A-17**

- `actions/{decide-submission,open-evidence,record-update-service}-action.ts` (new, `"use server"`) — thin by rule: parse the form, hand it to the connector. The audit subject is the **submission's** nanny read from the ledger, never a form field.
- `components/{VerificationQueue,QueueTable,SubmissionPanel,VerificationReference}.tsx` + `lib/{load-verification-queue,parse-queue-query,parse-decision-form,refuse-admin-action,nanny-name-of,format-london}.ts` (new).
- `/admin/users?tab=verification&open=<submissionId>`; the Sydney `VerificationTab.tsx` and `IDCheckModal.tsx` (direct `createAdminClient`, legacy columns) **deleted**; `/admin/verification-reference` re-pointed at the London read.

**`comms` — `admin_notifications` gets its one writer (ADR-160)**

- `notifyAdmin({ kind, subject?, summary, dueAt? })` on the seam, idempotent on `admin_notifications_one_open_per_subject_idx`; `src/boot/admin-notification-insert-row.ts` (new) is the service-scope insert. `call-layer`'s booking notice and the barred decision both take it, so REVIEW-3's re-verified "no module owns `admin_notifications`" pin is closed at the connector rather than per-caller. Suite: `comms.admin-notification.test.ts` (3, RED first).

**The crons (ADR-161)**

- `/api/cron/send-delayed-emails` now has a handler: `08.43` `callDueSweep` (finally scheduled — a Phase 1 debt), `scheduling.expireHolds`, `verification.sweepStaleProcessing` and `verification.sweepReminders`.
- `/api/cron/vetting-expiry` now has a handler: within `VETTING.expiryLeadDays` ⇒ `vetting.expiry-approaching`; past ⇒ `expire_verification_section()` ⇒ `vetting.expired`.
- Suite: `verification-crons.route.test.ts` (3, RED first, one of them a pin).

**Pins: 2 added, 2 flipped, 0 bent.**

- Flipped **by behaviour**: REVIEW-3's two `rls-isolation-conjunction` pins (ADR-162's predicate turns them green with no edit to the assertions).
- Added: `connections.hold.pin.test.ts` — the hold's **write** at K-row creation is `connections`' side (02 §4.2 row 7); `ConnectionRecord` carries no held pair and `upsert_connection()` inserts none. **Owner: `2d` / `connections`.** The **release** at L4 is built and proven in `int.rpc-0023`.
- Added: the `08.20` delivery of queued `email_logs` rows in `verification-crons.route.test.ts` — `comms` has no template renderer, so a delivery loop would only settle rows `failed`. **Owner: Phase 4a (`08.01`).**

**Design decisions worth carrying**

1. **The admin _is_ the cross-check, so the decision writes the outcome.** `stub-manual` reads no document and extracts nothing; 03 §4.4's cross-check "waits on the admin's typed surname". Modelling the cross-check as a separate later write would have invented a second decision nobody makes.
2. **`p_required` is an argument, not a constant in SQL.** `VETTING.requiredChecksByLevel` stays the one home of the policy; the function validates the shape it is handed and refuses an emptied L2–L4 list rather than granting a level. ADR-153 is reversed by one config line, exactly as ruled.
3. **The hold is silent because the level is the mechanism.** No second flag, no second copy path: every pool read goes through `nanny_visible()`, her sections say _sent · being checked · in review_, and the level line is neutral. Nothing on S-N-09, the hub, or in a message names a hold.
4. **The queue's authority and its subject are two different reads.** The session says who may act (`requireRole('admin')`, aal2, plus the limiter); the ledger row says whom the action is _about_. A form field that named the nanny would be an oracle, which is exactly what ADR-145 closed on the other two surfaces.
5. **One predicate, one home.** C-1 existed because the same sentence was written three times in three languages and one copy was wrong. `nanny_visible()` makes the next divergence impossible rather than merely unlikely.

**What the next unit must know**

1. **The hold's write at K-row creation is `connections`' and is pinned.** `sync_nanny_verification_state()` **releases** held rows at L4 and counts them; nothing sets `held_for_verification = true` when the K row is made. `2d` or the next unit on the K rows owns it (`connections.hold.pin.test.ts`).
2. **`0023` is not on preview or production.** `0021`, `0022` and now `0023` are all unapplied there — B-45 is BAI's, and the eight commands are in the `2b` entry. Every claim in this section was measured against the **local** stack forward from empty.
3. **A non-barring adverse disclosure has no reject reason of its own.** 03 §4.2's `adverse` reads "barred-list / adverse disclosure" and 03 §4.3 says "adverse → barred", so **every** `adverse` rejection bars and suspends. A caution that should not bar needs a second reason — 03 §12, BAI, with B-19.
4. **`verification.override` stays refused.** It is 03 §4.3's arm for a provider that is not a `ManualDecisionProvider`; none is bound, so no road reaches it.
5. **The Sydney users tab is still Sydney.** This unit deleted only the verification half (`VerificationTab`, `IDCheckModal`); `page.tsx`, `UsersTab`, `UserDetailDrawer`, `ContactUserModal` and the leads panel still read legacy columns through `createAdminClient`. `09.10` is `2d`'s or a fix unit's.
6. **No admin-assisted identity route** (04 §10 item 32, a11y-5) — the admin attaching evidence on a nanny's behalf needs an upload road on the admin side; the wizard's is the nanny's own.
7. **The decision form's `expiresAt` is a raw ISO instant** — a date control is a design pass.
8. **One signed-URL TTL for every kind of evidence** (`security-reviewer` L-5) — the DBS certificate image reveals at the same 1 h as a selfie. Splitting the key is 07 §6's, not a build unit's.
9. **`adminOverview` reads the whole ledger** (`security-reviewer` L-6) — the header's counters call `listSubmissions({})` unbounded. The fix is a counts-by-status read on the store, which is a 03 §4.2 contract change.

## Files created / modified in the current unit (2d — the nanny's own screens, the nanny's name on the parent surfaces, the K-row hold write and S-N-01's variant; L-008 Phase 2)

**Migration `0024_connection-hold-write.sql`** (+ its rollback twin) — one function body replaced, nothing else.
`connection_requests.held_for_verification` / `held_at` have existed since `0007` (R-14) and `0023` proved the
**release** at L4; what nothing did was **set** the flag, because `0019`'s `upsert_connection()` names its INSERT
columns one by one and the pair was not among them (`jsonb_populate_record` read them off `p_columns` into
`v_in` and then dropped them). `0024` adds the two columns to the **create branch only**: after creation the one
writer is the L4 release in `sync_nanny_verification_state()`, so a later K row must not restate a fact it does
not own, and the verify block asserts that the update branch never assigns either column. No schema change, no
grant change, no policy touched — and therefore, per **ADR-165**, no security clause to keep forward-only: the
twin is a clean inverse and its header says exactly why, with `service_role`-only, `SECURITY DEFINER`,
`search_path` pinning and `0007`'s CHECK all asserted from the catalogue on both sides.

**Verified on the LOCAL stack, forward from empty, and said so.** `supabase db reset` applied `0000`–`0024` from
an empty database (25 migrations, clean); `int.rpc-0024` then ran green (8); the twin was applied to that same
database and **4 of the 8 turned red** — the feature genuinely gone, measured rather than argued — and `0024` was
re-applied, green again. `npm run types:generate` produced **no diff**: there is no schema change to drift.
`0021` / `0022` / `0023` / `0024` are all still unapplied on `bb-ldn-preview` (B-45 is BAI's) and **no claim in
this section rests on preview**.

**Kickoff debt 2 — the nanny's name on three surfaces; one read for the two parent ones.** `matching.publicNannyName(auth, nannyId)`
reads `nanny_public` by id at **session** scope and answers a first name or `null` — 07 §5.1 rule 4 keeps contact
detail out of that view, so it is a name and nothing more, and no service-role use joins 07 §5.1 rule 5's list.
None of the three surfaces may import `matching` (01 §2.3) and all three may import `connections`, so boot injects
it as the `nannyNameOf` port and it leaves by `connections.nannyNameOf`:

- `positions` rail rows 4-6 — `ConnectionSummary.nannyFirstName` rides in on the summary `connections` already
  hands `positions`, so **no new import and no new port**: "Meeting with {nanny} — {day} {time}", "Met {nanny}",
  "You're going ahead with {nanny}", "Hired — {nanny} starts {date}". Row 6's name comes from the connection the
  **placement** names (I-3: exactly one), not from whichever summary sorted first.
- S-P-08's cards — the name heads each card and the stage phrase sits under it; the phrase is 04 §6.2's own
  vocabulary and the name is deliberately not folded into it.
- the admin call drawer — a nanny-commission row read `Nanny commission call · nanny <uuid>`; it now reads her
  name. **Not through this read**, and the reason is structural: see the `security-reviewer` section below. It
  uses `admin-verification.nannyNameOf` (`user_profiles`, keyed on her `auth.users` id, session scope with RLS
  as the second gate), which already existed for this question and falls back to the id's short form.

Every line keeps the nameless form it had when the view has no row for her (isolated, below the pool, gone). The
**fourth** caller — `call-layer`'s `aboutNanny` on S-P-01's "about {nanny}" line — is a **pin**, not a copy: the
value is only ever set from a C-c cascade payload nothing in production fills.

**S-N-17 `/nanny/profile` (`03.22` Rejig) and S-N-21 `/nanny/settings` (`03.24` Rejig).** Both read her own rows
and `verification.getStatus`, and nothing else. The Sydney pages they replace read `user_profiles`, `nannies`,
the Sydney check columns on `verifications` and `child_client` through a **service-role admin client in the page**;
the Sydney nanny-profile component moved to its one remaining caller, the admin impersonation viewer, which is
still Sydney's (`2c`'s gap 1, `09.10` open).

1. **S-N-17 is a view, not a second editor.** 04 §6.3 gives it two states and one exit (incomplete → S-N-18), so
   every "Edit" is a link into the stepper at the step that owns the field and `update_nanny_profile()` keeps its
   one caller. A second write road would be a second place for `03.18`'s completeness rule to drift from the
   database's.
2. **S-N-21's contact fields are S-N-18's own location step**, rendered in place through
   `saveNannyProfileStepAction` — one schema, one action, one 07 §8 row 16 ceiling, no new limiter row, no new
   entry in `check:action-limits`.
3. **The payouts section is dropped** (N-2; T-2.5, ADR-027): no ledger, no badge, no figure, and the suite
   asserts it rather than trusting it.
4. ★ **Neither screen names the hold** (ADR-157). `nannyVerificationSummary` is the one thing either screen says
   about verification and its whole input is the level `getStatus` answered — **read, never derived** (`2c` rule
   1. — plus the four section statuses. A nanny at L3 whose connections are held reads **exactly** what a nanny
      mid-check reads, because nothing is given to either view that could tell them apart. The suite sweeps every
      level × every section status and asserts that nothing in the rendered output says hold, held, withheld or
      hidden — including the word "hold" in the suspension line, which was reworded to "we've paused your account".

**Kickoff debt 14 — S-N-01's active / passive variant.** The under-3 signal N1 captures and never shows her
(04 §4.1 row 5) lives on `nanny_leads.lead_signals.under_three`. The read is widened rather than the signal moved:
`nannyAccountStore.get()` answers `worksWithUnderThrees` off her own lead row, keyed on the `lead_id` the account
already carries, asked **only** when there is one, at **service** scope because `nanny_leads` is service-role only
— a fourth named use in the module README (07 §5.1 rule 5), answering one boolean and nothing else off that row.
`addChildPitchCopy` is the copy side, pure: **active is the default** (absent means "we do not know", which is
every pre-signal account and every invited nanny), and **neither variant is a gate** — form, heading and offer are
identical, only the framing moves.

**The hold's decision, and where it is made.** `checkPreconditions` already read the nanny's facts for K-1 / K-2 /
K-3's verification floor; it now **returns** them, so the hold is decided from the same read — one read, two
decisions, no way for the two to disagree. A row created without facts is **not** held: the module never invents a
hold it has no evidence for.

**Test / doc bookkeeping.** `connections.hold.pin.test.ts` is **retired, not flipped**: its claim ("the module does
not write the pair") is now false, and it could never have turned green on its own because its assertion was made
against a hand-built record literal rather than against the module. Five live claims in `connections.inside.test.ts`
replace it. `matching/README.md` had two stale prose claims — it said the pre-check blast was unbuilt and pinned
`it.fails` in `matching.autofire.test.ts`; the blast is built (ADR-136, the `PrecheckBlast` port) and that file
carries no pin at all. Both corrected against the code (ADR-123 rule 2).

**Review battery: `security-reviewer` (ADR-142 — this unit adds routes). 1 CRITICAL · 0 HIGH · 0 MEDIUM · 1 LOW;
both fixed in-unit, 0 recorded, 0 pinned.**

- **★ C-1 (CRITICAL) — FIXED, RED first. `0024` is what made ADR-158's hold reachable, and nothing on the read
  side enforced it.** `0016`'s parent SELECT policy does hide a held row — `int.rpc-0024` proves it — but only
  for a query run **as the parent**, and `dbConnectionStore` reads at `{ scope: "service" }` by design (`0007`
  gives `connection_requests` no client write policy and the cascades run as `system`, with no session to read
  under). So RLS never fires on the application's own reads: `connections.forParent` returned the held row,
  S-P-08 rendered a card for it and the rail rendered rows 4-6 from it — and `2d`'s own new name lookup put the
  nanny's **first name** on those lines. The state it fails on is the everyday one the feature exists for (L3:
  above `MATCHING.minVerificationLevel`, below L4). It was inert before this unit, because until `0024` the flag
  could never be `true`; this unit is what activates it, which is why it is this unit's to close.
  **Fix:** `visibleToParent` — one rule, one home, applied at the **two parent-facing** consumption points
  (`loadParentConnections`, and `railRest` before rows 4-6) and deliberately **not** inside
  `connections.forParent`, because the machinery must keep seeing held rows: P-7's close cascade closes them with
  everything else (a filter there would leave a live connection behind a closed position that nobody can cancel)
  and K-1's duplicate and pending-cap checks count them. Defence in depth on top: `forParent` does **not look up
  a name at all** for a held row, so a future consumer that forgets to filter still cannot name her. The rule is
  stage-blind — a held row is hidden at every stage, terminal ones included — and **absent is not held**, so
  every row written before `0024` still shows. Seven claims in `connections.held-invisible.test.ts`, all red
  first, plus the note at `closeLiveConnections` saying why that one call site does not filter.
- **★ The admin drawer's read was silently wrong, and the reviewer's "no issues" on the name lookup is what
  surfaced it.** The parent surfaces' read is `nanny_public`, keyed on `nannies.id`. 03 §3.2's subject for a
  `nanny-commission` booking is `{ kind: 'nanny', nannyId: UserId }` — her **`auth.users` id** — and
  `nanny_public` deliberately carries **no `user_id`** (07 §5.2; the ADR-103 review's M1, pinned in `int.rls`).
  A lookup by user id against that view matches nothing, silently, on **every** row: a fallback that always fires
  and looks like a working feature. So the admin drawer uses `admin-verification.nannyNameOf` — `user_profiles`,
  keyed on the user id, session scope with RLS as the second gate (07 §5.2) — which already existed for exactly
  this question, and `admin` may import `admin-verification` (01 §2.3). **The planner's ruling said one read for
  all three; it holds for the two parent surfaces and cannot hold for the admin one, and the reason is
  structural rather than a matter of taste.** Recorded here rather than worked around.
- **L-1 (LOW) — FIXED.** `NannyAddChildPitch` carried `data-pitch-variant`, which maps 1:1 to the under-3 signal
  04 §4.1 row 5 says is captured and **never shown to her** — it would have put the signal in her own page
  source. The attribute is gone; the variant is asserted on the pure `addChildPitchCopy`, where it belongs.
- **What the battery confirmed:** the `nannyId` handed to every name lookup is server-derived at all three call
  sites and never client-supplied; `0024` changes no grant, no `search_path`, no policy, and its UPDATE branch
  cannot write or clear the held pair even with a stale value riding along in `p_columns`; `0007`'s CHECK cannot
  be half-satisfied from either the SQL or the TypeScript, because every write path sets both fields or neither;
  the widened `nanny_leads` read is keyed on the caller's own row and cannot be steered to another person's lead,
  returns one boolean, and degrades to "unknown" (⇒ the active wording) on every failure; both new routes derive
  everything from the session's own user id and render nothing to an unauthenticated or wrong-role caller; and no
  attribute, response field or copy branch on either nanny screen distinguishes a held nanny from an unheld one.

**Long functions.** Every file this unit touched that carried an `eslint.long-functions.json` entry had it
**removed and the function actually split**: `db-connection-store.ts`, `connection-preconditions.ts`,
`create-connections.ts`, `create-connections-slice.ts`. Nothing new was added to that file.

### Superseded — the handovers `1i` was written against, kept for the trail

**`1h` / `1i` must know (from `1g`).** (1) **The event you were told to consume is emitted.** `placement.started` fires from L-1b, every time, **with or without** a payments binding: `openDfyAccess` is an injected port and `wire-placements.ts` deliberately wires none, because a fail-closed binding would take a nanny's recorded first day down with it. Wire it there, one line, and `placements.inside.test.ts` already pins both halves. (2) **Nothing is charged at placement**, and that is asserted as a negative claim on L-1 (ADR-094; fix: offer-1) — a payments trigger added to L-1 will fail that test, which is the point of it. (3) **Rail rows 7 and 8 read `pending` and are yours.** `journey-rows-4-to-8.ts` composes 4-6 and leaves 7 (`payments.getAccess`) and 8 (the child-linking read model) named, not guessed; they are two functions in one file. (4) `paymentDueAt = startedAt + 7 d` is **not** set by L-1b — the row records `startedAt` and emits, and the sweep that raises the bill is `payment-due-sweep`'s (ADR-094).

**★ The next database unit owes one function, and three stores are waiting on it (from P1-STORES).** `positions` is a db store now, in every environment, so the two halves agree about where a position lives. What is **not** closed is the write: ADR-127 makes one unit of work one RPC, `guard-unit-of-work-query.ts` replaces `insert` / `update` with a refusal for every table reached under a `{ uow }`, and every position write runs inside the caller's unit of work (`create-positions-slice.ts` `commit`; `create-positions.ts` `amend` / `recordPrecheck`). `0006` / `0017` / `0018` define **no** `SECURITY DEFINER` function for `nanny_positions`, so a committed position move is refused at the port. **The same gap sits under `1g`'s `db-connection-store.ts` and `db-placement-store.ts`** — both write their tables as table writes and pass the caller's `uow` through too. `0019` therefore owes `public.upsert_position(...)` (the shape is written out in full in `boot.test.ts`, "what 0019 owes": `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path` pinned, `EXECUTE` to `service_role` only, the owner-bypasses-RLS assertion S5b's review added, `nanny_positions` **and** `position_schedule` in one transaction with the compare-and-set on `version` `advance` already sends) and the same treatment for `connection_requests` and `nanny_placements` — with an `int.rpc-00NN` **functional** suite beside it, because S5b's lesson was that 151 metadata assertions passed over a function that could not write. Pinned `it.fails` on the real wired port so it is flipped, not rediscovered.

**The nanny's side of a connection is closed (ADR-136, P1-STORES).** `Recipient` is `{ userId } | { email }`: a caller names a person and `comms` resolves the address inside its own send, from `user_profiles` keyed at service scope, logs it on the `email_logs` row and never returns it. So K-1's `connection-requested` and autofire's `precheck-nanny` both send, and **no business module can obtain an address at all** — stricter than the rule it replaced, not looser. Two things follow for whoever writes next: (1) anything **building** a `Message` is unchanged (the `{ email }` branch), but anything **reading** `message.to.email` must narrow; (2) `matching` reaches `comms` through the `PrecheckBlast` port composed in `src/boot/precheck-blast.ts`, never an import — 01 §2.3 still gives it no arrow, and the send must stay on that side of the seam.

**Two more, smaller.** (a) **A nanny's name appears on no parent surface** — not on the rail's rows 4-6, not on S-P-08's cards, not on the admin drawer (`1f`'s own gap). 04 §7.1 prescribes "Meeting with {nanny}" and "Hired — {nanny} starts {date}"; the lines carry the time and the date instead, because no connector puts a person behind these reads and a raw id in front of a family is worse than no name. One connector method fixes all three surfaces at once. (b) **`08.43` is written but nothing schedules it** (`admin/call-queue/lib/call-due-sweep.ts`, where 03 §3.5 seq 5 puts it). 01 §4f puts the schedule in the `send-delayed-emails` pass and `run-cron.ts` still answers `no-handler` by design; a cron unit wires it in four lines.
